Clear ideas As on many other occasions, our Father wanted us to ask questions. And they soon began to come. Father, how did St Nicholas begin to be an intercessor in the Work? One day I was in the Patronato Real de Santa Isabel, of which I was Rector: practically all the Rectors there would end up holding important ecclesiastical positions. I was getting ready to celebrate Mass, and I had tremendous financial difficulties. I said: As St Nicholas is the saint who solves financial difficulties, and the saint who gets those who are not able to marry, to get married… If you get me out of this, I will appoint you Intercessor! But before going to the altar, I repented and added: and if you don’t get me out of this, I will appoint you anyway. The financial worry was great; materially it was perhaps little; but today it would be equivalent to a number of millions. Father, was it solved? Where would you and I be if it hadn’t been? Under a tent and a few bits of tin! But I do not ask for miraculous solutions. First, I ask that we work, and that we maintain ourselves with that work, and when we can’t, I ask God to lend us a hand. I am not charismatic; we have to use the human means and at the same time the supernatural ones; they always go together. One has to behave like that with everything. For instance, nowadays there are people who talk about religion without having first studied anything. They call themselves progressive, when they are in fact regressive: they repeat the same errors that the Church rejected centuries ago. One needs study and the grace of God to be effective. Afterwards he alluded to a project of his, which he had talked to us about in the previous months: a book entitled Dialogue, in which he was proposing to discuss contemplative life in Opus Dei. There is a huge amount of material: thousands and thousands of notes. But I don’t want to make resolutions that perhaps later I might not be able to fulfil. I cannot materially achieve everything. If I myself do not make use of those notes before I die, Don Alvaro will look after them, for he is the best one to do it. Regarding something raised by one of those present, our Father told us that the following morning the contract to buy Cavabianca would be signed. At that time, next to the buildings of the Roman College, another was to be built for the work of St Raphael. Years later our Father decided that it was better if the students of the Roman College helped with the Centres of Rome, which is what is happens now. In 1968, however, the original idea was still there; that is why someone asked this question: Father, will the work of St Raphael be with people from all countries, or only from Italy? There will no wall to separate countries; on the contrary, I want to pull down all the walls that are put up in the world, except the walls of cloistered nuns. You already know that story about Tarazona. They were having a procession and on the way they came up against the walls of a convent. The sacristan said they would have to turn back, because the papal bull forbade entering the cloister. And the townspeople answered: Tarazona does not go back even if the papal bull commands it. We laughed heartily. When peace was restored, Our Father addressed don Vicente, who had twisted his ankle a few days earlier – I think he had been playing sport – and asked him: How is your foot? Look after yourself; you have to take many steps around the world.

Regarding old devotions Shortly after the beginning of the get-together someone produced one of the olive branches which the Father sends the Counsellors for Easter as a sign of unity. While it was being passed around from hand to hand, our Founder explained this Custom, which had started a few years earlier. It is from a tree they say I planted… We planted it among twelve of us, at least. Your sisters look after it carefully and, during the winter, they cover it with cellophane so it doesn’t dry up. The branches are still taken from the olive tree planted by our Founder in the garden of Villa delle Rose, which is the site of the Roman College of Holy Mary, in Castelgandolfo; on each branch there is a red seal of the Work. During those years, some people had started to question the value of many popular devotions. Our Father suffered because it showed an evident loss of Christian feeling. It was along those lines that questions were asked in the get-together: What do you say about novenas? Our Father’s answer came very quickly, reaffirming what he had already written many years previously in The Way: Who says that to do novenas is not a pure and clean sign of Christian piety? I have nothing against them: it depends on the person who does them. If they are the fruit of personal piety, I think all novenas are wonderful. What do you mean when you speak about assaulting Tabernacles? It is not a physical thing, my son, but a moral one. It means entering into different tabernacles by desire so as to keep our Lord company. I have done it many, many times… During those years when I used to go from one place to another to visit poor and sick people, or to give catechism classes to children, whenever I passed a church my heart went to the Tabernacle and I mentally prayed a visit to the Blessed Sacrament or made a spiritual Communion. I acted in the same way when, on my way, I passed by a non-Christian school or a non-Catholic church, so as to be very close to our Lord and make acts of reparation. That is real ecumenism, and not what some do now who do not treat Our Lord in the Eucharist well in order, so they say, not to offend those who do not have the complete treasure of our Faith. We always feel the desire for universal peace, for fraternity among all men; we are not fanatics, but we won’t confuse truth and lies. What does it mean in The Way, when it says that we always go to Jesus, and we always return to him, through Mary? That we shouldn’t feel too ashamed to go back to the Lord if at time we have offended him; and to do that, the best thing is to go to Our Lady. It is the same as when we were little and we did something naughty: we went first to our mother to be forgiven, and she went later to our father. So that is the way we should behave in our spiritual life. Someone else insisted: Is our Lady the best or the only way to go to Jesus? The Virgin Mary is the best way to go and to return to the Lord, my son. I do not dare to say it is the only one, but certainly the best, and certainly the shortest. I always have a kind of concern for those sons of mine who live in environments with a Protestant majority. I am afraid that as if by osmosis they might acquire the coldness that Protestants show for the Virgin Mary and lose the tendency to love her or be able to go and return to Jesus through her. Without having recourse to her, it is more difficult to have a relationship with the Lord. You have been vaccinated against those errors, thanks be to God. Don’t think that those who say something else are true theologians. We have to take the love for our Lady and devotion to the saints with us everywhere. I feel hurt by the lack of Christian teaching that is behind the removal from the calendar of so many feasts of the saints and of the Virgin Mary: those of our Lady, for the reason I have just given you; those of the saints, because they have been creatures like us, and their example encourages us, not to behave like them, but to imitate Jesus Christ. I have a great devotion for the spirit of the early Church, in which devotion to the Mother of God and to the saints is to be found.

Nurturing theology Taking the opportunity of the topic of devotion to our Lady, our Father started to talk about a great desire of his life: that there would be many sons of his dedicated to developing theology, bringing together theological science and piety. He made some comments on doctrinal deviations which were spreading in some sectors of the Church and of the work that was awaiting us in the field of theology. I hope that in Opus Dei there will be many outstanding theologians, who will prepare writings that are very good, solid, attractive, based on the doctrine of the Faith, not on what is contrary to the teaching of the Church. In many places, unfortunately, theology has become a kind of mummy as theologians neglected or abandoned their studies. They limited themselves to repeating the same thing, as if work on the science of God had come to an end centuries ago. And it is not true: one has to make continual progress in the understanding and exposition of revealed doctrine. But from that to describing as theologians people who write just about anything, while ignoring the Magisterium of the Church, there is a huge abyss. True theologians have to be pious and humble. It is indispensable for them to be able to recognise their mistakes and admit honestly: ‘This is wrong; I have made a mistake.’ Because in so many places there are some who call themselves theologians, and they are more like animals who eat acorns. My sons, when science does not go together with virtue, it ends badly. A theologian has to behave like a man of God: a man of prayer, who loves humility, who, when he finds difficulties in his intellectual work, knows how to apply his reasoning, while knowing that he will never manage to give a complete explanation of the divine mysteries. If God were to fit into our heads, how small he would be! Or we would be like God: a tremendous mistake. If we were to admit such a disgraceful presumption, we would fall into the deceit that the devil made our first parents fall into: eritis sicut dii. You will be like God. What a mistake! Humility, my sons! It is only common sense, even from the human point of view, to bow our heads before God’s Majesty. Throughout my life I have heard I don’t know how many theories about electricity, and many have collapsed, one after another. If natural mysteries have such depth that we cannot explain them completely, how much more must it be the case with the divine mysteries. We are men, limited men. That is why, to do theology properly, together with science, one has to cultivate piety very deeply: to be souls of prayer. And humble prayer! There were still more questions in the get-together, which – as I said at the beginning – was rather long. But perhaps they would be better left to another moment, so as to be able to meditate with greater serenity on the words contained here.

7 April 1968 (II)

Holy Week 1968 had a special atmosphere. For the first time in Rome, a meeting of students from various European countries had been organised to study typical university problems. Together with this meeting, promoted by ICU, an international St Raphael seminar was being held, which would produce many apostolic fruits. That is why it’s not surprisng that a great part of the Palm Sunday get-together was centred on this. From all corners of the soggiorno questions came up about apostolate and proselytism.

From all places Father, what should we do to get into the lives of others? Just as you have been doing; it’s an open secret. You will give witness with your normal, ordinary Christian life, and by going deeper in your studies. In second place, by respecting everyone, not being fanatical, but without giving in on matters of doctrine. Finally, when you are asked – and you know how cleverly to bring about the right opportunity – explain in a clear and simple way the true doctrine of the Church, and always with a smile. And how do we treat a friend who has no faith, but has many human virtues? Pray for him. If he has human virtues, there’s a good chance that he will respond generously to God’s grace, which nobody is lacking. Pray for him, but don’t tell him; treat him with affection and understanding. In answer to a question about apostolate with artists, our Founder mentioned the work done by some of his sons in that milieu. A lot of work is being done in some places. In Spain, for instance, there are actors and actresses who, after a performance, go to a certain church to attend Mass and receive Holy Communion, before going back home. There is no human work that cannot be made noble and sanctified, unless it is sinful in itself. It was also mentioned that a lot of good can be carried out with a pen, writing novels, or plays… Nowadays people read a lot; but – unfortunately – there is a great lack of criteria among novelists. Think how much good could be done by a good Christian writer. Without any kind of prudery or priggishness, and by allowing his faith to imbue his intellect and way of saying things, he can give a Christian perspective to all the issues that are raised in his novels and plays, showing clearly the kind of person he is, being and knowing himself to be, a son of God. Such literature can do an immense amount of good.

Love for freedom Our Father made use of this opportunity to talk about the pluralism that exists in the Work and the freedom we all have in temporal questions. Nowadays there is a lot of talk about pluralism. We are not surprised, because we have always practised it. We think it is reasonable and just that there be a diversity of opinions in all that God has left for men to discuss freely among themselves. But matters have to be studied first; in this way you will never be bigoted. Consider that we all have the right to make mistakes. If you realise that the error is yours, rectify honestly and carry on. Today I received a visit from a journalist and I told him: I am not receiving you as a journalist, but as a friend, because I love you. I made him see that in human matters, opinions depend a lot on the point of view of each one. I shaped my hand in a certain way as if to say: You see? For you it is concave, and for me it is convex. If I put myself where you are and you where I am, it would seem different. So then what? Can’t one develop a human ideal? Yes, my sons. One has to live up to a human ideal – each one his own, the one he thinks best – and know how to defend it, but without imposing it on others, considering that the others can also be right. And if in some case we think the other’s opinion is more likely, there’s no difficulty in accepting it and changing one’s opinion. This does not mean letting one’s side down; rather, it means behaving as honest, reliable people. Our Father looked around. Seeing our faces eagerly attentive to his words, he added amusingly: If someone doesn’t agree, that’s up to him. But he would be silly, because this is all very clear. It is not politics, but common sense. Then he continued: Whoever resorts to violence to defend his ideas, shows that he is not right. Don’t argue, for that is silly. Study the matter calmly, put your reasons down on paper. I have always recommended this. When people who like to argue put their arguments down in writing, they learn to speak in an orderly way and succinctly. That is the way to clarify matters, and it becomes easier to understand one another. Or not to reach an understanding, because it is not always possible to agree in all matters of opinion. What does it matter if the other person thinks differently to me? Why should I be an enemy of one who does not share my opinion? I am talking to you of the philosophy of rights, my sons; this is not politics. There is no room for single parties in my head, and even less in the life of the Church.

Facing Holy Week Holy Week had started. At the beginning of the get-together someone dared to ask our Father if he would give us a meditation during those days. My son, our Father answered: I do nothing else, when I come here to be with you! If you do not realise this, I am sorry… Whatever we say, we say in God’s presence: they are supernatural thoughts. The Lord knows very well that I do not speak to you about anything else. On the other hand, if I were to come down to give you a meditation, what would that group of young priests, your brothers do, who have my same spirit? It is not that I can’t, it is that I shouldn’t. I dedicate that time to writing, and in that way you all benefit. When I was younger, I found it difficult to speak; now that I have reached sixty-six, the ideas come easily. One after another and what is harder is to keep quiet. The get-togethers with our Founder – I remember them well – were moments in which we would drink in the spirit of the Work from the source itself: they helped us be better, they made us meditate, they opened up wonderful panoramas…, and we also had a good time, with the Father’s cheerfulness and good humour. In the one I am describing now, at the start of Holy Week, he helped us to go deeper in the mysteries of the Passion of our Lord. Read the Gospel, trying to use a version with notes, and enter into the scene. Do your meditation with it and try to be one more character in the scene. I have given away many Lives of our Lord and Histories of the Passion, because I am convinced that in this way, meditating those passages again and again, with the grace of God, one comes to know Jesus Christ well and react as if we were living close to Him. Through his most Holy Humanity, led by our Lady’s hand, you will somehow distinguish the Father, the Son and the Holy Spirit. You will get to know the Unity and the Trinity of God, and you will become a little bit mad, madly in Love, which is a good thing. Father, how can we live Holy Week better? Each one can live it as he likes. It can be sorrowful, glorious or joyful, according to the way each one has of contemplating the Passion. But for everyone it must be done in a loving way.

With total confidence We followed our Father’s answer trying not to miss a word. The way to treat others of the Work and the trust we should have in them occupied a good part of the rest of the get-together. I have complete confidence in you; it doesn’t cost me any effort. And have they deceived you sometimes, Father? Yes, how many times? Few. Have you cried? Yes, and I have suffered. Have you repented of the way you have acted? No, I prefer to be deceived by one than to mistrust a thousand. I do not mistrust any son of mine. While I live, I will trust in God and in my sons. Father, are you happy? With you, happy if you try to be good and holy, yes. Not with myself. I have 92 nothing; I only adorn myself with the plumage of others. Escrivá is a poor man. The ones who are good and holy are the children of Escrivá. I am ut iumentum. He also talked to us about those who had whistled recently: They are the ones who need to be looked after most: I have written to you many times about them. Treat them in the same way that mothers treat the new-born; with all possible care, with all the necessary attention. Not the way they say the Maragatos do – it is not true, but they say this about them – that the first night they place the newly born on the balcony in the cold air, because if they don’t die then, not even a ray of lightning will affect them… A mother suckles her baby frequently, she cares for it, she cleans it… That is what has to be done from the first moment to those newly born to life in the Work. Near the end of the get-together, he again insisted on the need for interior struggle every day. We have to try to strive for perfection in human things, because if not, there will be no perfection in what is supernatural – which will always be limited because we are limited, although we must try not to commit errors. I find myself full of imperfections, and I have been fighting for so many years. If you are dragging some defect along with you, I excuse you, as long as you are fighting to get rid of it. You must serve others without them noticing that it is a service: it should look natural even if it costs an effort. To do something grudgingly, would make it no longer a service to your brothers, because you would have made it clear that they owe you something: you are showing them how much it costs. Perhaps foreseeing that this wonderful time of conversation would not last much longer, for the gettogether had gone on for some time, someone tried to slip in a question in case it would be the last one. Father, why is the seal of the work the way it is? Ask don Alvaro; if he wants he can tell you… It is a very modernist thing: the world and the Cross inside the bowels of the world. We have to give thanks to God for having made us so simple and yet so profound, so old and yet so new, because the Cross is now two thousand years old. Someone still slipped in a question on how to build a relationship with the Holy Patriarch, and our Father answered: I am in the habit of asking St Joseph to teach me how to relate to Jesus, as he related to him, affectionately, intimately. Can’t you imagine it? He would hold Him in his arms, and cuddle Him and sing Him the songs one sings to children, with a tenderness that moves us and shows us how we should treat Him ourselves. And this is not an invention of mine. He asked someone to hand him a book with the preparatory and thanksgiving prayers for Mass which his children had given him some time before. Holding it in his hands, he read the prayers about St Joseph. That is how that get-together finished, at the start of Holy Week 1968.

4 April 1969

Holy Week 1969 is associated in my memory with the celebration of the second international St Raphael congress in Rome. These gatherings of students from different countries started in 1967. That first journey, the real prehistory of the present UNIV, was made by a small group of students from various Centres of the Work in Germany. As well as attending the liturgical ceremonies of Holy Week in a Roman Basilica, they had a get-together with our Father which they were delighted with, and were keen to repeat the experience. Our Founder suggested that the idea should be extended, and so the first St Raphael congress, which included university students from nine European countries, took place in 1968. In 1969 the number of those who attended had increased: eight hundred university students spent their Easter holidays in Rome and were received in smaller groups by our Father. For those of us who lived in Villa Tevere, which then also included the Roman College, the congress gave us the chance to get to know and speak with young men from a number of different countries, some of whom we had put on St Joseph’s List. Walks through the city and get-togethers with the people of the Work from different countries made it possible for us to learn many things about St Raphael’s work on the five continents.

An unforgettable memory On Good Friday some of us had a brief get-together with our Founder in the soggiorno of the fifth floor of Uffici. It was the first time, during the years I had been in the Roman College that I had been with him there. That very particular circumstance brought clearly to my mind a series of details. It was just after half past nine when our Father entered the soggiorno. He came from the oratory of St Michael, where he had been accompanying Our Lord at the altar of repose. He spent a few minutes listening to the rehearsal of some hymns we were going to sing during the liturgical ceremony that afternoon. When it ended, our Father expressed how pleased he was for the affection and care with which we were preparing the ceremonies. By contrast, he spoke to us about the situation in some places in the Church, where some strange things were being done as a result of a badly understood aggiornamento. Now that there are some people who are bent on destroying the Sacred Liturgy, I like to see worship performed with all possible solemnity. When this is not so I get very upset, for it cannot please God. Divine worship should be as solemn as possible: this is how the Lord wants it and deserves it to be. Those who want to simplify it, substituting sacred vessels with ordinary secular ware, do not give due worship to God. In the meantime the word had gone round that the Founder was in the soggiorno of the fifth floor. Fr Henry, who had come with the group from England, and Benito, who had come with the Belgians, and a number of others also attended the get-together. Once we were all seated, our Father spoke to us about the events we were celebrating during Holy Week, above all, the institution of the Holy Eucharist. During these days we have to give thanks especially to Our Lord for instituting the Holy Sacrament of the Eucharist, whereby he has remained amongst us. It is marvellous: he had to leave us, and yet he wanted to stay with us; and as he is all-powerful, he worked this great miracle of love. We cannot do some things we would like to do because our abilities cannot match our desires. However, Our Lord can: He went to heaven and he has, at the same time, remained hidden under the species of bread and wine. We have to thank Him for three things in a special way: the institution of this great Sacrament, its continuity by means of the words of consecration recited by the priest, and its administration. They are three marvellous expressions of God’s goodness, which fit the needs of our nature. I always think of the love of a good mother, who cleans her little one, washes and perfumes him and then, with many kisses, says: I could 94 eat you! The Lord has said this to us as well: Take and eat me! He could not be more human. But we do not make God Our Lord human when we receive him; it is he who divinises us, exalts us, and lifts us up. I make bold to say that the Lord would not have done well if he had not acted that way, knowing our weakness, knowing that we are inclined to sin. Towards the end of the get-together, he referred again to Eucharistic worship, which is an expression of our love for Jesus Christ. The Lord wishes us to worship Him with the greatest possible majesty, because this befits our condition as poor creatures, which should be expressed and reflected clearly, without stinginess, in the liturgical ceremonies.

Love for freedom Someone mentioned that a young man had decided to whistle after a get-together, and our Father – as on so many other occasions – took advantage of this to insist that in the Work we are very great friends of freedom. I give thanks to our Lord for our vocation as people who work in the middle of the world, to sanctify it and to sanctify themselves, who really love and defend people’s personal freedom. A freedom with responsibility, I insist, because otherwise it would not be liberty but licentiousness. How much peace there would be in the world if everyone willingly accepted his personal responsibility! He explained that, although maturity is not merely a matter of age, because correspondence to grace plays a very important part, a minimum of age and experience is required to take important decisions in life. I don’t like people to come to the Work who are too young. It is better for them to have knocked their head a bit against a wall, and had time to see themselves as no more than a worthless rag… There followed stories from the congress, in which there had been some non-Catholic participants. Someone asked about ecumenism, and our Father explained some fundamental points of Church teaching. He talked about the affection with which we in the Work always treat non-Catholics, without giving in on matters that belong to the deposit of faith. Jesus Christ founded one Church only, and he promised it the constant assistance of the Holy Spirit. The truth cannot change. That is why those who are outside will have to leave behind what gets in the way and accept the complete truth if they really want to achieve the union of Christians. Soichi asked what advice he should give Kazuo, a Japanese resident of the Colegio Mayor Albayzín, who was thinking about his conversion. Tell him to be a great friend of freedom, to allow himself to be led by the Holy Spirit, to ask God for the light of faith… The people of the Work will never push him: he will find himself totally free. Without freedom, we are not doing well. However, if you saw that I was not doing well, you would have to tell me. Sometimes a person can become confused and has to be helped with fraternal correction; if not, when the moment of confusion is over, tears are shed, and perhaps then it is too late. Perseverance in the Work is wonderful. And insisting on the love we must always have for freedom, he concluded: Soichi, always put yourself on the side of freedom. Faith is a gift of God. Let us pray for him; I will do so also. You and I can help him a lot with prayer. Afterwards, if he is baptised, let him receive Confirmation, so that he will immediately receive divine fortitude.

A great social work I don’t know how the topic came up of the need farm workers and people living in the countryside have for formation. Now that schools for families of farm workers and country folk are a wonderful reality in many countries, I’d like to recall what our Father said in that get-together, when this work was just beginning. Your brothers in Spain are working very well in that area. They are setting up schools for the sons of small landowners. As well as providing the necessary human formation, because grace is based on nature, they give them Christian formation, and a specific professional formation, so that they can achieve high yields from the little bit of land they cultivate, and can thus maintain their family in a dignified way; and this is facilitated by forming cooperatives so as to be able work the land using modern machinery. This can significantly reduce the migration of large numbers from the countryside to settle in poor areas of large cities where they sometimes lack proper housing, water or light, and where they lose what little knowledge they have of religion to become a multitude that is easy prey for communism. He told us also that our sisters were also going to begin this work. Your sisters, some Numeraries and Associates, are looking at some of the work they’re doing in France. With the experience there and what we have in Spain, they will also start to work with women from the countryside. While he was telling us this, we could notice his fatherly pride in that apostolate, which the Lord will make use of to stir so many souls. It is a colossal, indispensable, work. The rural population emigrates driven by economic factors. First, people have to be provided with a minimum of well-being and then spoken to about faith, because the supernatural is based on the natural. Thanks be to God, the majority of those people received Christian formation when they were little. That is why, though they may have lived many years away from the Sacraments, if you scratch a little, the faith soon appears. And he concluded: It is a work which is extremely good, with which we do not gain anything material, but we please God immensely. Perhaps it was the reference to the incomparable value of spiritual goods, which suggested to our Father the importance of detachment in our ascetical struggle. The fewer things we have in this world the better. In this way, life is a wonderful fight for peace, and a person who has too many clothes on is likely to lose. One has to be detached, to have only the exact amount, and only just enough. But only enough does not mean to be dirty: it should be noticed that where we live, there is the warmth of a home; if not, we have got it all wrong.

Peace and war Bright sunlight filtered in through the windows. Lowering his voice, our Founder began to speak to us about that war of peace which our Lord wants us to wage. A war in which at times we will be wounded, but from which we will come out victorious if we do not allow ourselves to be separated from the Lord, who is our strength. Be humble, so that our errors do not take us away from God. Love, when it is true, is humble. It’s the same with human love; when a marriage breaks down, it is because one of the couple, or both, are proud. Our parents would not have been able to put up with one another if they had not been humble. The fact that God has all the perfections, and we don’t have any, must lead us to say: mea culpa! The experience of our weakness will make us humble. Our Father looked for a few seconds at the ceramic on the fire-place: a group of men on horseback with shields and weapons, ready for combat. Our Founder read out the words underneath it: Pax in bello! The same is written in the large loggia of Villa Vecchia. Ours is a war of peace, but a war that will last until we die. Do not worry: if we are humble we will always come out victorious, and we will be called victors. But not if we are proud. And if we lose sometimes, we will go back to God immediately, like the prodigal son of the parable: Lord, in spite of this defeat, I love you. And peace and joy reign again as well as the joy of fighting again with greater determination.

Reasons for joy Referring to the deep joy that comes from being united to our Lord, our Father told us how happy he had been when the font in which he had been baptised was sent to Rome. I consider it an act of God’s providence and a caress of his that I was given what remained of the baptismal font where I became a Christian. I did not expect it; it was a very pleasant surprise, and I am very grateful. It had been destroyed by the communists, out of hatred for religion, during the Spanish Civil War; only a few fragments could be recovered. The rest has been reconstructed. The font was originally flatter and lower. In the oratory of Santa Maria we raised it up a little by mounting it on a capital, which also came from Barbastro cathedral, like the others in the Aula. That is where my mother, my sister Carmen and my other sisters were baptised. That is why, when it arrived here in bits and pieces, I was very moved. Another question brought to our Founder’s mind the construction of the buildings of Villa Tevere. Then he spoke about the bureaucratic difficulties that had to be overcome to begin the works in Cavabianca, the future site of the Roman College: A lot of money, which we do not have, is going to be spent there. But, as always, the Lord will help us. Once the City Council approves the project, since your architect brothers are working very hard, it will go very fast. You will have more room to take walks. The oratory will be based somewhat on the one in Pozoalbero, but in the Roman style. The tabernacle will be in a separate chapel, but will be visible through an opening in the reredos. This way liturgical ceremonies can take place without having to turn one’s back on our Lord. The reference to the liturgy brought to our Father’s mind again the difficult situation the Church was going through. Any cold comment, every gesture of indifference towards the Spouse of Christ, hurt the heart of our Founder deeply. The Second Vatican Council taught us that in the Church many things were rotten; rather, not in the Church, but among some ecclesiastics. The Church is not rotten, because it is sine macula, sine ruga; it is the spouse of Christ, without any stain or wrinkle. I say this to you because it is public knowledge and, also, because it is good to know the symptoms of an illness so it can be cured. God’s grace is not lacking, but good will is also necessary. The times we are in are serious; we have to pray for the people who have lost their way, and for ourselves as well, because we are weak, so that God may hold us by his hand and we don’t do anything stupid. Our Father joked with José María, twin brother of Juan Luis. He commented that they were identical, like two drops of water, except for their side whiskers: those of Jose Maria were first-class. When perusing the account of that Holy Week which appeared in our internal publications, I could see that the get-together of that Good Friday morning was like a summary of everything our Father told the participants in the St Raphael congress, whom he strongly encouraged to love the Church more and to pray for the Roman Pontiff.

10 April 1969

We had left Bilbao by train on 2nd April. In Barcelona we took a ship that would take us to Genoa. Rome was the final stage of our journey. The expedition was made up of students who would soon finish high school in Gaztelueta, and three teachers, all very keen to be received by our Father. And that is what happened in fact. It was in 1969, when the meeting at Easter was only like the seed of the marvellous event that we now have the joy of experiencing. But groups from nearly all the European Regions had already been there. We arrived in Rome at the end, when many of the others had already gone back home. I do not know if that was our good fortune. The fact is that half way through Easter week, we had a get-together with our Father. I still vividly remember the event, which lasted a long time. The first words of our Founder made explicit reference to the school, which both students and teachers were moved to hear. Gaztelueta began because it was necessary to continue involving more parents in the human, spiritual and doctrinal formation of their children, and because – on another level – it was necessary to confirm the need for lay Christians to work professionally in education, basing their work on respect for the freedom of the pupils. In Opus Dei we have a great love for the freedom of others and also our own. I would not have the right to defend my freedom, if I did not make the effort, with the same determination, to first defend that of others. We are able to reach God with the help of grace, but freely, because we want to; each one therefore lives with great personal responsibility. Never forget it, my sons: you are free, but you are also responsible. With freedom and with responsibility, we Christians cannot stop at what is comfortable. We must go for what is uncomfortable, for the love of God and for the love of others. This does not mean that we must upset anybody. I have many friends who are Muslims, Protestants, Jews… And I try to treat them always with affection. Why not? I try to bring them closer to Christ, freely. I like them to know my Catholic Faith, and also that I am deeply convinced that it is the true Faith; but if they do not ask me, I don’t tell them anything. Look at the example of Jesus Christ. He came to teach: coepit facere et docere; first, with his example, afterwards with his words. If you open the Gospel, you will see Christ always in dialogue with the whole world, not only with the disciples, but with sinners, and hypocrites, the Pharisees, and with the poor. But when he speaks, he teaches and gives doctrine with the authority of a Teacher. Many people talk a lot now about dialogue, as if it were a recent invention. And there, in the Gospel, you have it written how the true conversation of a teacher and friend should be. It is nothing like what some do, who try to outdo each other in saying the most silly, absurd things. The Father was surrounded by a great number of young boys. Some had already whistled, and were his sons, but others also felt the paternity of our Father, and were not unaware of what they owed him. However, it was our Founder who started thanking them. You make me feel youthful by your presence: thank you! And now I will ask you for something: that you pray for me, so that I am a good son of God, that I may be faithful to my vocation – at least, like a good husband is to his wife and a faithful wife to her husband – so that I may always be cheerful, until the hour of my death. After this aside, he went back to where he had left: If a person struggles to accommodate his life and his behaviour to his convictions, even in the midst of errors, he merits my respect. Is he a heretic? Is he a pagan? He merits my respect equally! Then, Father, don’t you wish his conversion? Of course I do! with my whole heart, because I am not a hypocrite, and I believe I hold the truth while he is in error. But I respect him and love him with my whole heart. One of the boys, perhaps moved by what the Father had said, referring to not stop being content with what is comfortable, asked how to react when faced with mediocrity. There will always be mediocre people around you, and you yourself will feel mediocre at times. We men are made of clay, are not worth much. Normally, we won’t have the chance to behave in a heroic, spectacular way; however, the Lord wants us Christians to walk at his pace, with a heroism that does not stand out, with hidden self-denial, like Jesus in his thirty years of hidden life. In the workshop of Nazareth, he felt his heart burn with zeal for souls. He felt a very deep desire to express his great proof of love: giving his life to redeem us. However, he behaved as filius fabri, the son of the artisan. Therefore the things that make a lot of noise are not big. Have you understood where mediocrity is to be found? The boys – and the teachers – followed the Father attentively. His doctrine was not new to them; it was what we had tried to teach them at school. But our Father said it with a warmth and brilliance that was new: new, because his words were spoken with unusual vibration; new, because his holiness and affection helped us discover aspects which we had not suspected before. Work, my dear children, ordinary work is – for a normal Christian – the basis for holiness and, therefore, for happiness. Do not let your imagination run wild with strange things, with little miracles. If you go to a restaurant and ask for a fish, and they bring you a snake, your reaction will not be that of a miracle-worker who with a simple sign of the cross converts the serpent into a fish. Like that, according to the spirit of the Work, no-one would become a saint. The one who is served with a snake tells the waiter: Will you do me the favour of bringing me a fish, because I have not asked for what you have brought… In any case, if that were not possible, one could possibly think of mortifying himself, and eat what has been served… But don’t forget, saints have reason too, they have common sense! Then, continuing with the theme, with his special gift of tongues, our Father added an example to illustrate another fundamental aspect of the spirit of the Work. When, referring to the women who do the cleaning at the University of Navarre, I say that I don’t know if their work is as important or more as that of the Board of Governors, I’m not play-acting. I’m simply repeating what I have always said. The task of one of those women who goes about cheerfully and does everything with love, can be heroic, and not at all ordinary, and certainly more effective than that of a great researcher who only thinks about publishing his studies. I insist, which is of greater value? It depends on the love and sacrifice with which one’s work is done, but with willing sacrifice, cheerfully wanting it. If not, it is better not to do it. One of the boys asked how one could have greater confidence with one’s spiritual director. You go to the spiritual director willingly; because you want to. If you don’t want to, it is better not to go. God, with this and other traditional means, helps us to live in a Christian way; he offers us a hand from Heaven, but we must want to hold that hand. I advise you to choose a fixed spiritual director, because it is very difficult to be a good judge of oneself. And a proof of your confidence will be for you to tell him everything, and speak with him things about your interior life with complete sincerity. Our Father explained other basic aspects of the spirit of Opus Dei. All honest human paths can be divine; whether single, married or widowed, we can become saints. Earthly things in themselves are good, but they can be converted into evil; it depends on us. Father, what about human love? I bless it with both hands, when it is a clean love, like the one of my parents, and yours. I will give you a clear rule, which I have been giving for more than thirty years: you can go out with a girl, and be completely sure of not offending God, if you behave with her in the same way as you would in front of your mother. Your mothers know a lot about love; if not, we wouldn’t be in this world. I rejoice when thinking about the joy of my parents when they were expecting a child. We are the fruit of love; and in the case of a Christian couple, the fruit of human love and love for God. The Lord has asked me for my whole heart – and you fit in there as well – but I never get tired of saying that marriage is also a divine way.

To sanctify marriage, those who are led by God to follow that vocation – who will be the majority – have to prepare themselves, by guarding their senses, and keeping their bodies clean. Don’t you see all the misery and unhappiness around? It seems as if some people do not bring children into the world, but bits of rotten flesh. It is so wonderful to think that in future you can ask of the one who is to be the mother of your children, to live a clean, pure life, just like you. A man who doesn’t live a clean life cannot expect his wife to be a saint. Then, those whom the Lord asks for more, should not be foolish; they must be ready to give themselves, and will discover a happiness that is not possible to describe with words. Large families give me great joy. But when I find a marriage without children, because God has not given them any, I am also filled with joy: not only can they sanctify their home just the same, but they will also have more time to dedicate to the children of others, and there are many already who do so with touching generosity. I am proud to assure you that I have never quashed a noble earthly love; on the contrary, I have encouraged it, because it has to be a divine way, every day more so. Someone said he had hoped to find more visible signs of a religious spirit in the centre of Christianity. This is very possible, my son. But in any case, if you are observant, you will have noticed that there is no corner in Rome where you don’t find an image of the Virgin Mary. If you see signs of brutal, aggressive paganism, turn your eyes in another direction, and carry on. Pray and make acts of atonement. Father, and might this not be due to the crisis in the Church? Look, my son… When I was your age, I also protested when I saw what seemed an injustice. We have all protested at some time. In any society, the young are important, because they constitute the future, and whoever has a minimum of common sense will know how to foresee the future, by taking good care of the young. If they tend to protest, you have to remember that this has always happened. It is only that before we did not give ourselves importance, saying to one another: we are the best, the only authentic ones, as some of your companions might presume. You have to learn how to be understanding and be ready to excuse. You often have a reason for protesting, and you should be listened to; sometimes, they won’t be able to satisfy your demands, simply because you have not taken into account other circumstances which have escaped you; and there will also be moments when you are not right at all, and you have to be honest enough to admit it. In any case, a protest without the desire to find a solution is a sterile protest. I liked the following question especially, above all because of the boy who was ready to ask it. Father, how can we show our gratitude for the formation we receive at school? A good question. God Our Lord will ask you to give an account of the formation in freedom you have received. A slap on the face was all we got in my times. Only one teacher attempted to punish me with a slap, and I escaped by running away. Some days later, I was with my father in the street, and that same friar came to meet us. I thought: My goodness!, Now he will tell my father… In fact he stopped, and said something pleasant... and left without saying anything else. I was so grateful for his silence that I pray for him every day. Afterwards, he died a martyr. In my time, education everywhere, especially in lay schools, did not try to foster a sense of responsibility among the pupils. On the other hand, in all the schools where formation is given according to the spirit of Opus Dei, you are loved, you are understood – although sometimes you may not think so – and you are taught to reason. This is why the teacher tries to become your friend. Father, said one of the boys, I am an American from Gaztelueta. Do you know what I tell you? Smart fellow! May God bless you, my son. You have to take back home this spirit of Opus Dei, this spirit of freedom, of responsibility, of freedom, of pluralism, which we have not learned from the Second Vatican Council. When the Council Fathers stated that all human ways can be marvellous, they didn’t reveal anything new for us. The Lord wished Opus Dei to teach this forty years earlier; and they said then that this priest was a heretic, a madman, and many other compliments. In spite of everything, I cannot be an enemy of people who do not think like me. The sign we must wear, those of us who have known the spirit of Opus Dei, is the plus sign which, on the one hand, is the sign of the Cross of Christ; and on the other, the symbol of unity, of making other people brothers. Next, one of the teachers asked: 100

Father, what do you say to the teachers? That I am very happy. Is that enough? Because we sow the basic spirit of Opus Dei in people all over the world, in all social spheres, in all environments, and we give them the ascetical means to conquer in the interior life; and we teach them that, if they fall sometimes, what is important is to go back to the Lord always. And what about us who are amateur teachers? That’s not true: in Opus Dei we do not work as amateurs, in the ordinary and disparaging meaning of the word. You say it out of humility, but it is a humility which is not properly understood; it is not good. I don’t like it, you must reject it. I know that you don’t allow yourself to do shoddy things for God Our Lord, that you give him the best you can. Because of this alone you are a professional, and that makes me happy. The get-together practically finished there, although there might have been other questions. The boys were delighted. We had brought our Father some paintings made by the best artists of Gaztelueta, and the Father gave us in return a gift that amused everyone; a magnificent Mexican hat. When he left, our Father said again: I thank you for having come to see me. Just seeing you fills me with joy, and makes me feel young again.

28 March 1971

Tomorrow will be the forty-sixth anniversary of my priestly life. You can pray for me, my sons, because I have not made good use of my time. But I am very sorry and have great desires to make better use of the years I have left. It was a Saturday, 27th March 1971. Sixteen years have since passed, but the memory is still fresh. However, I have the feeling of calling up very old events. Ever since our Father went to Heaven, it is as if time has accelerated, making the historical proportions of everything to do with the person and teachings of our Founder much larger. That morning, when we came out of the oratory after Mass we heard that the Father was in the soggiorno of the Casa del Vicolo. We had a quick breakfast. The unforeseen get-together was a preparation for the feast of the following day. The Father referred several times to the following day’s anniversary. Do you know what it is to have served God for forty-six years with my many weaknesses? They are years that the Lord will judge. We remained silent, without daring to interrupt the Father’s prayer. Can’t you think of anything, my sons? Remind me: Father, he will not be the Judge, he will be Jesus…You don’t know how to say it, you rascals! Later on, Quico asked a question on the same topic: Father, how does one feel after forty-six years of being faithful to the Lord? In spite of calumnies, defamation, persecution – while I am alive, you will not know these things – I feel immensely happy, very happy! It is not from me, it is God’s. I spend the whole day giving thanks to the Lord, for everything, even though I feel capable – I have said this many times, and it is not play-acting – of committing all the errors and all the horrors that the most wretched person can commit. But even so, I always live happily. There are no human reasons for this joy: only to be with you, although now I don’t see you often. It is natural: You have to look after your things, and I mine, my own work; the opposite would be disorderly. The expressive gesture of our Founder, when we finished that short get-together, helped us prepare the feast of 28th March. Stretching out his arms, he told us: I need God’s grace, and the affection of all of you: the affection of my sons sustains me. I live like that, with my hands wide open, asking for the alms of your prayer; I will do so especially tomorrow. Can you imagine what forty-six years of priesthood are? Sometimes I like thinking about our Father in Heaven with that eloquent gesture, asking me for prayers and fidelity. And at the same time – I learned it from him – I think that he holds out his hand as well for me to grasp it firmly, to be assured and strengthened, so that I can count always and in everything on his affection and protection.

A day of thanksgiving The sun rose on Sunday 28th March. From early morning we had been looking forward to the feast, which the get-together of the previous day made even more present. The first hours went by, with much prayer for our Founder and with our ears attentive to the telephone or the bell in Uffici which, would announce our hoped for get-together with the Father. At mid-morning we gathered in the soggiorno of the Casa del Vicolo, which had seen so many wonderful moments. When the Father arrived, we all greeted him and congratulated him. The Father was grateful, and asked us to keep him in mind. He asked us, Where were you, you fine fellows, forty-six years ago? Then immediately afterwards he said we were in his priestly heart, and in the inklings of God’s love, which he had felt so clearly a few years earlier. We sat around him. It is very good to give thanks to God, and it fills the soul with joy. But we have show him our gratitude always – when people say things are going well and when they say they are going badly. It never happens to us, because we know He is the one who handles the puppets, and knows perfectly well what he does. Let us thank him, not like a puppy, who after he has received a morsel, brushes against his owner’s legs for another one. No, not that. We have to be grateful also when the Lord, instead of a tasty sweet, sends us a hard knock. If you give God thanks for everything you will have made good progress in your spiritual life. And, also, the Lord will reward you with immense joy. Our Father prepared for that anniversary as he always did: considering God’s goodness and allowing his heart to express his gratitude. He passed on to us his way of living in God’s presence, and we in turn tried to engrave these confidences of his interior life on our hearts. Father, how can we give thanks to God for so many good things? I have just told you that we must be grateful. But how depends on each one. It is a very personal question… Our Founder was always very grateful. He was moved by little expressions of affection from his sons. In that get-together he referred directly to don Alvaro and don Javier: I am very especially indebted for all the charity of those who are very close to me.

Loving the Holy Mass The get-together started with that expression of gratitude that slipped out from our Father’s heart. But he quickly asked us to say something. One of the first questions recalled today’s event. Father what do you recall about the day of your ordination? Nothing that I should say now. But I would tell a lie if I did not recognise that I recall many things, I think I remember everything… In a few strokes, he mentioned a recent event that had caused him great sorrow, and ended by saying that we should love the Mass very much, which is always the unbloody renewal of the Sacrifice of Calvary. I have told you a thousand times that I am never alone when celebrating the Holy Sacrifice. Don Javier usually serves my Mass, but I know that, close to me, in those moments, are all the faithful, and the whole Church, because I act in persona Christi. Not only those who believe, but those who do not believe: all humanity and all God’s creatures during those moments when I am Christ. I want you to have clear ideas, my sons, and be patient. At times God allows the waters to get muddy, but they will flow properly again. They say there is a shortage of priests, and it is true; everyday more will leave, until the disorder is put right. These complaints from our Father’s heart always ended with a warm invitation to pray, and to make acts of reparation, and show more love for the Spouse of Christ and for the Roman Pontiff. The previous day he had also insisted: Love the Holy Mass every day more, my sons, also for those who do not love it. The Mass celebrated on the altar, on the ara alta… When I began to study theology, it was explained that the sub-diaconate did not impress a character, as it was not of divine institution, but now there are people who do not want any sacred orders at all. They try to confuse priests with lay people, and promote a priesthood ad tempus: with time for the altar and time to lose one’s soul. They do not believe in the word of Jesus Christ. Without an altar, without a Sacrifice, without a priest, without a Victim…, we would be like Protestants. You must love the altar, the holy stone – as they call the altar here – consecrated, and with the relics of martyrs or saints; and always be certain of the divine reality of the priesthood, and also that the Sacrifice of Calvary is renewed in an unbloody manner in each Mass.

Make lots of acts of faith, sons of my soul. We have the duty to atone to God Our Lord. Tomorrow I will celebrate the Holy Mass like every day, with the greatest possible solemnity: with Christ. Do you see how the ordinary is something extraordinary? We sometimes do not realise the value it has.

Unity and freedom The Sunday get-together continued with the intervention of Ernie, who had come to the Roman College from the United States, and is now working as a priest in Columbia, where he was born. He said that he had woken up at night, and he had immediately thought of the Father. I also wake up at night, normally a number of times, although I sleep enough. I then begin to pray, even out aloud. Not only Our Fathers and Hail Marys, but anything else that occurs to me. So you joined my prayer. You did well, my son. When I re-read this get-together, I cannot but recall our Father’s constant concern and prayer for the Church. And I also remember the advice I was given once in the fraternal chat, to live those moments in a supernatural way– which don’t happen often – when I find it difficult to go back to sleep: to pray for the Father, thinking that perhaps our Founder might be saying vocal prayers at the very same time. Father, someone asked, will many be ordained this year? Five or six groups of people look after that job, and they are already studying it. Aren’t you happy that in the Work there is no tyranny of any type? It is a great blessing, my sons! It is natural that we should behave that way. We are only in agreement about matters of faith, or what belongs to the spirit of the Work, which is also within the Catholic faith. If someone does not go along with our way of doing things, he leaves. Those of us who remain – which is practically everyone – are only in agreement on those few things. I will never ask for your political opinion. You can do whatever you jolly well like, as long as it is within the limits laid down by the doctrine of the Church. In Opus Dei, each one acts according to his personal conscience. You will never act with my conscience in temporal things, nor I with yours: I would not allow it. Long live freedom! A freedom that is based on and gives rise to a deep union, which a French paper called monolithic. Monolithic? Yes, with a total pluralism. Do not quarrel with any one because he thinks differently from you. If his point of view is mistaken, with respect to Christian doctrine, don’t get angry: treat him with affection, because, if not, he will never be converted … but be careful. I already reminded you recently about that parable of the prudent virgins who do not want to give their oil to the others. If dealing with such people were ever to present a danger to your faith, then … out! Never! Is that clear? We can give our oil, while keeping what we need for ourselves; but not more. But… no arguments. Don’t start arguments. Believe me: they are, at least, a waste of time. In the Work I don’t allow them. I have always told you that if you don’t agree among yourselves, each one writes a short note – you are not going to compose a book – and passes it on to the other. In this way you will see that you are in agreement on some points, and not on others. You will then respect each other, and rest in peace. The Father arched his hand. For you, this is convex; for me it is concave. And if I change the position of the hand, it is the other way round. There are many matters – which do not form part of the dogma or morals of the Church – that are like this: they depend on the point of view from which they are seen. If you don’t agree, at least you understand each other, and you go on from there. If a son of mine has the vocation of a tyrant, he should leave, because he does not fit in Opus Dei. That is very clear; we love the freedom of each and every one. Also that of those who do not understand us.

An episode in the Jenner Residence That lesson in understanding and affection for the world, and especially for those of the Work, was firmly engraved in those present. Another question emphasized this same point: Father, why do we love you more every day? I also love you with all my soul, my sons. It is natural that since we all form one body, I should not love this finger less, or this ear, or you, who are much more than my finger, my ear: you are my heart. 104

Now, recalling these words which moved us at the time, I think we continue being his heart on earth. How much our Father loved us and still loves us! We can explain that he joyfully accepted sufferings during his whole life, so that his sons could find the way easy and expeditious. Soon after the get-together began he had referred to those blows which we have to thank God for, because – as he assured us a few months before he went to Heaven – they are also affection and God’s providence. At another moment, our Father’s words helped us understand something of his suffering. One day, when we were in the Jenner Residence, after the Spanish civil war, Juan José Pradera – whom I had known for a number of years – rang me up: ‘Father, can I come and see you?’ ‘Come whenever you like, I answered. ‘Señor Gonzalez Oliveros will accompany me.’ ‘Come with whoever you want.’ ‘Give us a time.’ ‘One time is as good as another for me’ – I answered – ‘because I am going to be at home these days. Tomorrow, for instance, come whenever you want.’ They came, I received them. And they told me: ‘We have come on behalf of the Tribunal for the repression of masonry.’ ‘All right, why have you come?’ ‘Because they have accused you of being a freemason.’ Blessed be God! They have called me republican because I receive republicans; monarchist because I don’t reject monarchists; communist because I don’t close the door to communists; freemason because most likely I have received many and will continue to receive them. Some help us a lot, not because they are masons, but because they have a good heart. Why should I close the doors on anyone? I don’t have any temporal ambitions. You must act as your own conscience tells to you, with all freedom in temporal matters. General Saliquet, with others, formed part of the Tribunal for the Repression of Freemasonry. He was a man with a big moustache, the military governor of Madrid. They told me that when they heard the accusation that I was a Freemason and that I led an association of freemasons, he had asked: ‘But, are they chaste?’ ‘Yes.’ ‘Then they are not masons, because I do not know any who are chaste.’ As you can see, it was a special logic. May the military forgive me: it was a basic argument, but effective. Those two came to tell me, and to apologise. In the end Juan José asked me: ‘Father, could we see the oratory?’ ‘Yes, I am very happy for you to greet the Lord.’ We went in the oratory of Jenner, poor but homely. We knelt down, and suddenly Juan José said to me in a low voice. ‘Here is where they say you do miracles.’ ‘What miracles?’ I asked. At the time I had diabetes. Some forms of that illness leave the patient very thin, others grow fat. I became very fat. ‘They say’ – he explained – ‘that you seem to levitate in the air, because you place some electrical apparatus…’ We weren’t able to buy electrical apparatus… We were hard put to acquire electric bulbs! I started laughing, and answered: ‘Well seeing how fat I am, it would be a first class miracle…’ I did not defend myself against those accusations. I have never done so. And I don’t defend the Work, either; I only speak the truth. You must, you have an obligation, above all, with the tongue, which is a holy thing, although at times men turn it into something shameful. So rest assured that in what refers to me, if I hear some stupid remark, I shrug my shoulders. Afterwards I speak the truth, and that’s that. I did not defend myself. How could I show that I am not a freemason? It would be absurd if every time they accuse me of something, I have to prove I am not. I would 105 have done nothing else during my forty-three years of life in Opus Dei. No! I am not a little bull who goes for every red rag. I have often said that I am like a cow in the ring: I go for what I want, for the real target. And the target, my sons, requires patience, to offer things to God, with joy, giving thanks. After that, falsehoods collapse on their own. And when the occasion arises, speak the truth briefly, clearly and honourably, so that there will be no-one – even though not very intelligent – who would not understand, no normal person who would not recognise that that person is saying the truth.

Advice on how to be happy The get-together was long. Our Father asked us to tell him things, and joked with some of us. He commented on the difference between those who had been recently in Tor d’Aveia and the rest. Javier, who with Jorge was in charge of Club 91, told some amusing stories that made us laugh. Our Father again went on with what he had been saying to us. In these forty-three years of the Work, when newspaper men come to me – I only grant interviews occasionally – and say to me: ‘and you who have enemies…,’ I answer: Enemies? I cannot have any, because I like all of them a lot. I say this with all my heart as a priest and as a Christian. Out with enemies, because it is not true! There will be people who won’t understand me; perhaps I don’t understand them either, but in that case I will have the decency not to say so. Patience! I don’t expect everyone to understand me. What I want is that on that small portion which we all have in common, within the great circle of freedom, those of us in the Work will be in agreement. As for the rest, if they think the same, excellent, and if not, I respect them. I do not go into their house, and they don’t have to enter mine. It is so clear, so clear, although being so clear some people still complicate it. You must bear in mind that, from the beginning there were some holy men – sometimes blatantly, other times secretly – who got together to spread calumnies. All this is over; don’t be worried about any of these things. Smile, forgive and love: that’s the way to win battles. And continue to working. Never think you cannot make mistakes. I have made mistakes very often in my life, and if God were to let go of my hand, I would make more. But have the good will to want not to go wrong: that is why I listen to others, and tell you to be attentive to what they have to say. It is one of the most difficult things. Fanatics do not want to listen to the opinions of others. And what shall we do when these things happen? Pray, make allowances, forgive, work and shrug our shoulders. These contradictions are storms in a teacup. They pass, all of them. If only you knew. I have written many documents that will not be available until after my death. When you read them, you will say: How terrible... But I have always been happy. You will be too, if you remain faithful to your vocation. I am a poor sinner but I want to be a saint (…) Apart from that, I have written them down because, among other reasons, I have to give you clear ideas; it could not be otherwise. I want to be a good priest, a good Christian. And I feel happy. If I am a better priest of God, I will be happy, and if I am a much better priest, I will be much happier. This is the secret. “Look after number one”, and you will be happy. They said this...? Let them say it? They did this? They have done it so often! You cannot imagine: You will know some day. It has all been a trifle for me. ‘Father, don’t you feel these things?’ Yes I do, but one can suffer and be very happy. At this moment, I am not suffering from anything: I am happy at your side because you are faithful, because you are loyal…! God bless you, my sons. You have so much to do on earth! You must keep in your soul such a superabundance of faith that it will overflow into the hearts of others, into other minds. In this way, we will contribute to the peace of the whole world and the joy of the people of all backgrounds.

Clear ideas Then the Father began to say how crazy some people were who were trying to reduce everyone to the same level, when God has made us all different from each other. I am not the same as you, I am a sixty-nine year old man, with the characteristics of someone born in Upper Aragon, and a little bit of a Frenchman too; a little, I do have some French blood. Not tall, but normal stature: 1.71, if I have not shrunk. I know – he commented good humouredly – that between vertebra and vertebra there is a cartilage that flattens with age; that’s as far as my knowledge of medicine takes me. But I know what I don’t know, 106 outside the fields of Theology and Law. In other matters, I like to listen to the experts, although when they begin with points that are too deep, I say: Enough! I prefer to have faith in those matters. If I need a doctor, I go to him; when I need an engineer or an architect, I look for them… Why do I need to know about everything? It would be absurd. Men need human faith. If not, nobody would ask an architect to build him a house, or call a lawyer when he has to solve a legal problem, or go to the doctor if he did not feel well… Those who say they do not have faith in God place their faith instead in the science of any poor man they find. I was saying that we are not all the same. Here I see some are taller, others smaller, some with brown hair, others with more or less fair hair; each one has a different amount of blood. We cannot make men be the same, when God has made them different! It is absurd. Are they going to put us into a mould, and click! Even physiologically the same? It goes against reason. And not only physically. They can’t make us equal in intelligence, nor in memory either; and not to mention the will, which is so weak even in those with a strong will… It is God’s providence that wishes us to be very different. But certainly: all men must have a minimum of dignity in their family and social life…; and we have to try to ensure that this minimum exists. Whoever has talents should get ahead, even though his family is not well off. In the social sphere, I have no time for those hypocrites who go around the place living very comfortably while speaking about equality. You must do as much as you can – in your professional, social, national life, each one in his own country – to try and raise those who are lower, without pulling down those above, for they will come down on their own. To speak about class struggle is unjust, it is not Christian. I have not given you a particular theory. I am speaking in general. God forbid that I should enter into sociology! I am a theologian; I speak only about the things that refer to God. Is that clear?

A living tradition The get-together was about to finish. The Father addressed one of his sons sitting opposite him. Really, he was happy speaking to each one of us, and also to those who were spread all over the world, and even those who were still to come. You have to be very faithful, my son. In that way you will form part of the living tradition of the Work, for having listened to the Father. I am a great sinner, a sinner that loves Jesus Christ very much. When I was young I did not dare to say these things. Afterwards, I have told you so clearly. I have known a number of Popes: Pius X, Benedict XV, Pius XI, Pius XII, John XXIII, Paul VI. Cardinals, a whole lot of them. Bishops, eight regiments. Founders of Opus Dei there is only one, and you are with him now. He confesses that he is a sinner, and he speaks the truth; but when he speaks, he does so with a supernatural purpose, to form you. I will pass away when God wants – I ask him that I won’t cause you too much trouble when it happens

– and each one of you will be like a relic for your brothers. There will be a real, living tradition, because you will tell the others what the Father told you. Very often I tell you things forcefully, on purpose, so that they will remain fixed in your mind. Now that these things have been fulfilled, I like to meditate on those words of our Father, asking God that this living tradition of Opus Dei will never be interrupted. He referred to the difficult situation of the world we’re living in, and asked: What must you do? Pray and keep the faith in its entirety. Love Confession, which is attacked by many now, and Holy Scripture. Love all the sacraments. Love the Mother of God with all her privileges. Love Peter in the person of the Pope. Ask that the Father be good and faithful and that he may always keep his balance. It is what I try to do with you: and not be affected by external things, but always at peace, serene! For that, you must be loyal, and very keen to be good Christians, which is the same as being saints. The get-together ended with our Father’s blessing. In the evening, before dinner, he attended the Exposition and solemn benediction of the Blessed Sacrament in the oratory of the Holy Family. Together with don Alvaro and our brothers of the Centre of the General Council, those of us of the Casa del Vicolo were also present. The Roman College had celebrated the same liturgical ceremony, in the morning, in the oratory of Santa Maria, now the Prelatic church. 107 27 July 1971

During the last years of his life our Father remained in Italy during the summer months – except in 1974, when he was carrying out his catechesis in America. From 1965 to 1967, he worked in various places in the centre of the peninsula – in Tuscany and the Abruzzi – and in 1968, further north. In 1971, he was in Caglio, a small town between Como and Lecco, which is a few kilometres from Castello di Urio. As on other occasions, he was accompanied, by don Alvaro, don Javier, and Javier. As the conference centre was quite close, he went there several times to spend a few hours with his sons. On 24 July, the students of the Centre of Studies of Milan went there to begin their summer semester. Three days later, our Father came to see us. Although normally the get-togethers were held in the garden in the shade of a big magnolia, with a wonderful view of the lake, including those occasions when our Father came, that day we stayed in the living room. On 30 May that year, he had made the Consecration of the Work to the Holy Spirit; and so we weren’tt surprised when he began the get-together talking about the Most Holy Trinity. When we deal with one divine Person, then we begin to deal with the three Persons of the one true God. The Most Holy Trinity: to go down this deep is something marvellous. God is so great that we cannot understand him. Father, why did you make the Consecration of the Work to the Holy Spirit? Because our Lord wanted it. It will be the last one. The text is a little longer than the previous consecrations; nearly a compendium of theology. Your priest brothers will be able to teach a lot with that text. I suggest that you meditate and study them too: you will find many ideas to speak about the Holy Spirit. We had only been a few minutes in the get-together when someone proposed a song. To our delight there would be many songs these days because of the Father’s visit. Our Father made one condition: But an Italian song. I am at home wherever I go. Here in Italy, I am Italian. We sang Nascosta Rosa. We had practised it some days earlier in Milan, hoping that most probably we would have these get-togethers. I remember that ten years later, in 1981, we sang it again, also in Urio, when Don Alvaro came to see us in similar circumstances. When we had finished, he told us that our Father liked it especially, and that he had made some changes to the words: specifically, he asked them to change the title from Mystical Rose to Hidden Rose. When the song ended, someone asked about the virtue of humility, and our Father told that amusing story which, on other occasions, he asked Don Javier to repeat. Once they told me about the story of a friar who, with complete simplicity, said he had spent twenty-five years fighting to be humble, and he was very proud of having succeeded. We will have to fight against pride until the end of our lives. Pride dies twenty-four hours after we do. The days before the annual course had been very intense apostolically. Between the end of the exams, and our going to Urio, many apostolic plans had been made with the St Raphael boys, among them quite a lot of excursions to the mountains. At that time it was the custom to go by car to Champoluc, a place in the Aosta Valley, and begin climbing from there. There, high up in the Alps, a number of young lads were spoken to about giving themselves more to God. Father, how was proselytism done during the first years of the Work? The same as now. You have had brothers with a marvellous docility, who have gone to work in so many countries. I have made them move from one place to another like chess-men, and they have always answered Yes. I have sent them to Ireland to speak English; to Germany to speak German… That is the way that the Work has come about. 108

In Japan you have wonderful brothers. In a land where Catholics are a small proportion, they have opened Centres and corporate works without asking us to send them a cent from here – which we didn’t have, anyway. On the contrary, they sent money to contribute to the general expenses of the central offices. They have built a beautiful new Conference Centre, where people go throughout the whole year, perhaps pagans, many of them. He told us how much he had been moved seeing the generosity with which they were contributing, precisely from Japan, for the construction of Cavabianca. After that he stopped and, looking at us one by one, he commented: What a marvellous thing is our fraternity! We all form only one thing, we are of the same race: the race of the children of God! The secret of the Work is prayer, as I have told you very often: to pray, to receive setbacks joyfully.
