Normally they are only little things, like bees that are very small but together make marvellous honey. In our soul, setbacks are also like the work of these little insects; they are like a sting that pricks and hurts; but if we accept them for the love of God, they form a splendid treasure. Our Father spoke in Italian, as he used to when he was with his sons from that Region; but, from time to time, he would introduce a phrase or a word in his mother tongue. That is why, they asked him about Castilian as the family language of the Work. It was decided years ago in a General Congress taking place in Switzerland. All those at the Congress who did not speak Spanish presented a motion that in the Centres they should study the Spanish language, because they wanted to be able to read the writings of the Founder in the original language. I then told them not Spanish, Castilian, because besides Spain, it is what they speak in many other countries in the world. On 15th August there would be a new ordination of priests in Madrid. Our Father, as on other occasions, had written them a letter; but this one was a little longer, and he wanted it to reach all his sons who were priests. It was dated 10th June, the Solemnity of Corpus Christi. A few months earlier, the reforms introduced into the liturgy after the Second Vatican Council came into force. At that time, he spoke to us very much about the love we should have for piety, and especially in all that had to do with the Holy Eucharist. In the get-together, he used the opportunity to explain the significance of these new ordinations for the universal Church: If the sigh of the last little old lady in the dark corner of a church is a great good for the Church, because it is an act of love of God, think how much more will be the acts of love of the priest, now that there are people who want to do away with the sacredness of what is sacred. He had already spoken, somehow, about the topic that someone raised then: Father, how was the universality of the Work lived in the Work, when we were few? If you behave like a red hot coal that burns everything it touches, you will do the same as the first did. If not… it would be a pity. As many of those present were still young in the Work, and possibly had never heard him speak, don Alvaro referred to something that our Founder had mentioned on other occasions: the Benediction with the Blessed Sacrament at the end of the first Circle of St Raphael, in which Saint Josemaría not only saw the three who had attended, but three thousand, three hundred thousand… The Work began in Madrid, said our Father taking up the thread again, as it could have been born in Peking. It is universal. Another song; this time it was a solo sung by Diego. Then we talked about an apostolic initiative that had suffered from delays, caused by an injustice, that would soon open its doors. Our Father helped us to foster our supernatural outlook: Don’t forget that injustices accepted for love of God bring great good. God’s will is always good. God is a very wise Father, who loves us more than our earthly parents do. Not a leaf moves without his permission. Therefore we must always be happy. When we know we are children of God, there is no possibility of becoming sad. If sadness enters our life a little, one talks about it in the Confidence, one says everything – even if it might be embarrassing – and it disappears immediately. When I was little, my mother would tell me: My son, you should be ashamed only to sin! Shame is something of the devil: he takes it away so that we do what we should not do and then he gives it back when we have to talk about it. Sincerity is a need for the children of God in Opus Dei. Be sincere! Frater qui adiuvatur a fratre quasi civitas firma! We walk better with company than on our own; especially if we belong to the same set. I am capable of all the errors and of all the horrors that can be committed by the most depraved person. Therefore, I am not scandalized by anything. Father, how can we do apostolate in difficult surroundings, where people say they have no faith? Ask for the help of the Holy Spirit so that his fire may burn the souls of our friends. Repeat that marvellous aspiration: Ure igne Sancti Spiritus! The Holy Spirit carries out his work in all souls, also in those who do not have faith. I have many wonderful friends, who do not have the grace of faith. For example, I have received a telegram from a Jew who told me that he was on the point of opening an apostolic work of your sisters, which he has financed completely. Many of these persons receive Baptism or return to the Faith before they die. We must love them all. Father, what do you think about most these days? The answer was very quick full of affection, correcting that son of his, using a warm tone of voice. You have no shame! Do you want me to make a public confession? Our Father had come to be with his sons: it was relaxation for him. And we tried to make it a time for him to rest. There were more songs, which spoke of clean human love: we knew he loved them, because they helped him turn towards the Love of God. During a pause, he said: We are not afraid of human love, because we are in love. I have done my prayer very often with the words of these songs, which I like to sing with a divine meaning. We have to love the Most Holy Virgin Mary a lot. We also included a few songs of the Work; among them, Pero pesca submarina. All of us have been fished. Do you remember what the Lord said to the Apostles? Faciam vos fieri piscatores hominum, I will make you fishers of men. So that all of us in the Work are fish and fishermen… fishermen too. And the Lord has taught us that we have to go after the fish, as He has done with each one of us: he has chosen us from all eternity, He has always loved us infinitely, and has been infinitely patient with us: he went after us until he threw us the fish-hook of his love. In one of the places in the central offices I had an inscription placed: elegit nos ante mundi constitutionem ut essemus sancti in conspectu eius. From all eternity the Lord has thought about us and he has chosen us to serve all souls. It is marvellous: that is why I like to hear you sing that you want to be fishermen: piscatores hominum. Good! But the Lord always wants us to be free. You know that I am a great friend of freedom; especially in these times of so many lies. Now everyone calls themselves democrats, and at the same time, they lie shamelessly: they are incapable of respecting the freedom of others. This also happpens in some sectors of God’s Church. They respect the freedom of those who propagate heresies; on the other hand they tread on the right and the duty of teaching the true doctrine of Christ, that doctrine which is the one of always: quod semper, quod ubique, quod ab omnibus. The Lord has fished us, isn’t it true? So we must thank him by being faithful to our way, which is always personal. In the Work – I like to say it always that way – along that great wide road, which marks our spirit, each one has his own path to travel, and which he must follow with the grace of the Holy Spirit. We cannot think that others must follow our same steps: each one has to go his own way, along that wide road, and the others must respect it. Don’t forget this. The heart of the Father, at every moment of his life, has always been along that road, which is very wide. You, each one of you, will be led forward by the Holy Spirit, guiding you along your own way, according to your own personal needs, so that you can reach eternal life. Love the freedom of others a lot. For me it is a need. You will say: Then, Father, how can we influence the behaviour of another person? What has the Lord done! He has entered our souls without asking permission, for he is Creator and our Father. I love to speak about our God, about our heavenly Father. I would like all my sons to show a great love for their parents, and that you live the fourth precept of the Decalogue with affection and attention: the most sweet precept… because we should all show that love for our parents; but we should love our Father who is in heaven even more. We must all want many good things for our parents on earth; and, if they have some defect, we do not notice it: Do you understand? Well, in our 110

Father, who is in heaven, there is no shadow, no stain. He is all clarity, all beauty, all love. The greatest, the best thing, we can do in this life is to love God, who is our Father. Is that clear? Yes, Father! Of course we are paternalistic! For that we have a marvellous Father in heaven! Someone wanted to return to the theme of the net, but with a hidden intention. Father, how were you fished? This son wants me to confess in public. I would tell you if we were on our own. But this time, I will only tell you … I will speak in Spanish. Until then, the Father had expressed himself in Italian; to answer this very personal question he spoke in his mother tongue. I was about fifteen years old when I experienced that first inkling, that first presentiment of love – I don’t know how to explain it even now…, when I saw that God expected something of me. In Latin, a low quality Latin, I said to the Lord, like the blind man of the Gospel: Domine, ut videam! Domine, ut sit, Lord let it be what You want; I also asked the Virgin, our Mother: Domina, ut sit! I have repeated that petition many, many times. The ut sit! took rather a long time to receive an answer. Our Lord also took a long time to come on earth, after many patriarchs and prophets, just men and martyrs of the Old Testament, had invoked him with rectitude for a long time. I was then in Saragossa. He told us the story of that image of the Virgin of Pilar which he had found again after so many years, when he did not even remember it. They took it to Rome. As a proof of his faith and of his recourse to Our Lady, before he knew what God wanted, he had written on the chalk this ejaculatory prayer: Domina ut sit! How can we practise expiation? It is a need of our soul. It is the same as when sons want to make up for the insults and ill treatment that their mother has received. When one loves the Lord, it arises spontaneously. The Holy Spirit comes into our life, and leads us to make those small active penances voluntarily; and moves us also to accept willingly those other passive ones, which are even more effective, because they make us suffer whenever we receive them. We have to accept them always for the Lord: for Him, for his Love, with the desire to make reparation for our own sins, and the sins of mankind. We can all be good children of God, and we must make an effort to be so. Franco began a new question: Father, when we were in Rome for Easter… I would like to spend time with each one of you. I love you all the same, and sometimes I really have a bad time because I cannot give you an embrace… When you are many – hundreds and even thousands – I suffer tremendously because I am not able to tell each one of you what I wanted to say. There was a boy from Forcella, Naples, Franco continued. The Father kissed him and said: Come on, the work in Forcella is in your hands! This lad whistled recently, on the feast of Corpus Christi. Now Jesus has kissed him. Since the city of Vesuvius had been mentioned, Diego offered to sing a Neapolitan song which he had translated from its local dialect into Italian to make it easier to understand. There is nothing in the world comparable to the Love of God, to those intimate caresses of the Lord… I remember that, once when I spoke about them, someone had commented: perhaps we Catholics behave well because we expect a prize, a reward… It doesn’t matter, that is a good thing: it is the theological virtue of hope, which we cannot obtain by ourselves, that the Lord gives it us freely, like faith and charity. So we are in agreement, we await a reward from Heaven, which is the possession of Love, the greatest gift the Lord can give us. Don’t be ashamed to admit that you hope for this reward too.

* * *

The time had come to finish. A little later, our Father went to the car to go back to Caglio. We had hopes that we would see him again before the end of the Annual Course, and so it was. He came to the Castello again on 29th August. During the rest of those weeks, knowing that we were only a few kilometres from our Founder was a continuous stimulus to make better use of the means of formation and renew the desire to be very faithful.

18 October 1972

In the spring of 1972, we received the news that our Father would make a journey a few months later through the Iberian Peninsula. We knew that he wanted to see all his children in Spain and Portugal, and as many people as possible. But until then there had been very little experience of large get-togethers. It was difficult to imagine what that long journey would mean for the apostolic work in those two countries. That catechesis – as he began to call it – would be a very special caress of the Lord; a surprise prepared secretly. After spending a week in Pamplona and three days in Bilbao, he arrived in Madrid in the afternoon of the 13th October. On 15th October he had the first general get-together, in Tajamar. On the following day he managed to escape for a few hours to Molinoviejo. Gatherings were also organised for that great apostolic marathon on 17 October. In the afternoon of 18th October there was a get-together for the older members of the Work then living in Madrid or in other cities nearby. They gathered in the sitting room of Diego de Leon. Our Father would have liked to have spent some time with each one on his own. Often, when they were not present, he had talked about the veneration we should show towards the first ones who had persevered in the Work. They had come to Opus Dei when the whole building had still to be built, placing their youth and their whole life in the hands of the Lord.

The same pot A similar get-together to the one that autumn afternoon – with the same faces, the same place, with the same demanding and confident tone – could have been held thirty years earlier: nor was there a change in the stew-pot which the Father took the food from. He did not begin with anything new. He asked now what he had asked everyone in 1928: personal holiness and interior life. The one who does not do the prayer gets in the way. The one who becomes bourgeois and does not think about others gets in the way. The one who does not do a small mortification, at least, every day, gets in the way. The one who does not have an intense life of piety with a very great love for Jesus in the Blessed Sacrament, for the Most Holy Trinity, for our Mother in Heaven, gets in the way. The same stew-pot and the same demands, strong and clear. He was not speaking to little boys. He was addressing grown men, with many years of fidelity and struggle on their shoulders. Our Father knew this well, and precisely because of that he underlined very clearly the basic points of our spirit, as if he wanted to stress that we can never consider ourselves immune from the danger of lukewarmness or infidelity. That is why he continued to refer to other topics which he always put in first place when he spoke to his sons: to persevere it was necessary to practise the virtue of sincerity with great fortitude – savagely, if necessary. Let us go to the Virgin, Mother of God and our Mother. Without piety, we would always be stumbling along. We will go forward, with defects: Don’t worry. I do not blame you, and in fact it happens with me as with mothers: that, as I understand you, I love you even with your defects. Do not despair if sometimes you do something silly. Tell it! Let the dumb devil have nothing to keep him busy, because it would be a shame if at our age you were to behave like a six year-old. Better said, like fourteen year-old children, because six year-olds don’t know how to keep quiet, they say everything that occurs to them. The get-together was, as always, full of amusing comments, helped by the fact that our Father had known all those sons of his for many years. As soon as he saw Florentino – who had suffered a heart attack – he had to ‘scold’ him: Don’t give me a fright as Florito gave me – for without permission – he had a heart attack. You can’t do that, Floro!

That there may be nowhere to hold on to Four days earlier, in the afternoon of 14th October, a present arrived at Diego de Leon which had special value. It was a bell that had come from the parish of Our Lady of the Angels; one of those which our Father heard pealing on the 2 October 1928, while the Lord made him see the Work. I am very grateful for that token of affection of those who have been involved in this, he commented. I will take it to Torreciudad where I have proposed placing it beside the statue of our Lady in the esplanade. It will be possible to celebrate Mass there. We have taken lots of photographs of that bell. It is the only one that remains, because the others were destroyed during the war, and they melted them down to make cannons or whatever. I am very grateful to the parish priest and to my sons who have been involved in that gift. You already know that it is one of the bells they had in the parish of Our Lady of the Angels, which was practically on the outskirts of Madrid. The city reached Estrecho, and I would go even further in my visits to the poor and others. I am moved, even though I am so used to your affection. When you make me suffer, I am also used to it. Don’t worry, because if I am a father, I am also a mother, and I am obliged to bear it all with affection. But you are too good to me. At that time Torreciudad was being built, and our Father had offered our Lady the sacrifice of not going to see the works until they finished. He had only been there in April 1970, when they were had started the excavations; and he would not go back until May 1975, one month before he went to Heaven. At the same time as Torreciudad, they were also building in Rome that other madness – our Father’s last – which was so important for the Work: Cavabianca. The works were progressing well, but it would still take two years before the students of the Roman College would be able to leave Villa Tevere and go to the final centre. As I was coming up the stairs I met some boys who told me that they’re going to the Roman College. Marvellous! I told them. You will not be going there to become supermen; you will be men like everyone else, with defects, making errors and mistakes, but with the good will of being saints. Also – I went on to tell them – you are going to be very uncomfortable, in a room with five other people, which are the number that fit. But this will all be put right when we have Cavabianca. He was happy describing how much the material conditions in which his sons lived would improve. With that same joy, he made a simple description of the bedroom he used: in Villa Tevere, he had managed occupy a dark uncomfortable room on the second floor of Villa Vechia. It is a very tiny room, very pleasant, and the floor is still in good condition. You did it all so affectionately: it looked very fragile, but it’s lasting, isn’t it? he said, addressing Fernando Delapuente. Yes, Father. There is also a little table that belonged to the porter of il Conte – referring to Count Gori-Mazzoleni, the previous owner of Villa Tevere. Then there is an old armchair which always becomes unstuck, like the one they used to have in offices in the last century, and was very comfortable. You will have seen them sometimes, haven’t you? There is also one of those old chairs, which that man had stored somewhere: I didn’t use it all that much. At the foot of the bed there is a bench which, if you turn it over, you can see it has been mended with bits of wood taken from packing cases, because it kept coming unstuck. And that’s enough. He liked us to have whatever was needed in our centres to work and rest; serving the Lord with all our strength; but at the same time, he taught us how to be content with what is enough to live soberly and with temperance.6 That description was a clear example of his poverty and detachment. Ah, I had forgotten! He continued: There are two alarm clocks, as I call them. At the head of the bed there are four tiles with a cross, one of those with arms like arrows. There it is written: Jesus Christus Deus Homo, because I feel moved that Jesus Christ is perfect God and perfect Man. Then, opposite the bed, there is a very good crucifix, bought in one of those countless corners of Rome, which are like the ones in the Rastro – a street market in Madrid. It is gilded bronze, but very well gilt. On the door that leads to the

6 The Way, 631 corridor and opposite the bed there are some words written, so that I can see them when I am in lying down. Javi, what do they say? ‘Aparta, Señor…’ Set apart from me, Lord, what separates me from you. I need to be woken up; I need to go back to God every minute, like the prodigal son. That’s it; you know now as much as I do. More jokes and reprimands followed, with the Father’s going from the things of Heaven to those of earth without any distinction; very naturally, because he put the same heart into everything: loving God without holding anything back, and loving his children with his whole heart. During those longer moments of the get-together, someone who perhaps thought he was getting older asked: Father, what can someone do who has already reached a certain age and…? You don’t have a “certain age”, don’t get wrong ideas. Look, you have to fight in the same things that you and I have to make an effort with: being concerned about the others. Try to do that very well. Afterwards he said, that morning, someone had spoken to him about starting a university college; but the daring and the dreams of our Father always went further… I asked them: Only one? There have to be many. And what’s more, let the parents do them, which is only natural. We are not interested in anything for ourselves. What for? We have to fight, and fighters and wrestlers don’t wear clothes, so they can’t be grabbed hold of.

Kiss the stone and put it back in its place Our Father felt keenly his suffering for the Church and he spoke often of that constant battle which had been started by the enemies of the Spouse of Christ. It was like an epidemic that even affected organs which until then had seemed sound and healthy. Our Founder saw he was obliged to use all the means, and provide the necessary antidotes to prevent the sickness from affecting his children. That any error could be spoken of openly in any forum hurt him deeply. This was one reason why he had agreed to the idea of carrying out that long catechesis. He wanted to become a loudspeaker so that many people could hear the true doctrine of Christ, without any change or disfigurement. He could not remain silent. And so, a question on Eucharistic worship helped change the tone of the get-together. It is a matter of faith – and we firmly believe it – that Jesus is in the Holy Eucharist with his Body, with his Blood, with his Soul and with his Divinity. So that we must love him, deal with him, venerate him, offer our worship, receive him. There are so many people now repeating old heresies. There are none that are new! The devil is not very clever and has long since run out of ideas. Those who become the devil’s tongue and call themselves theologians, without being so, only say the same silly things as always. We must love, for those who do not love the Lord. We must be sure and firm in the faith, my sons! Faith cannot change; we have not made it like a democracy, as they say nowadays. The Church is not democratic (…), but hierarchic. Faith is a generous gift that God has wanted to give humanity; and the Church possesses that treasure, that deposit of faith, and cannot change it: neither increase nor diminish it. So, let us be firm in our faith of always, in what has been held by all Catholics. And let’s atone; for it is the moment for atoning. He said his lips would not gossip, and not in the large get-togethers of those days either. He would follow his custom of always – if you can’t praise, say nothing7 – and he asked all his sons to do the same. Referring to some people and to certain errors that were being spread, he said: One has to love them! What we have to do is to love them a lot. Love them a lot, my son, as I know you do. Carry on! And pray, because only in that way will we get our Lord to put an end to this great trial. The Church has never been so bad as now, because the enemies of the Church remain inside, and they are protected by other enemies of the Church who are inside and higher up. And what can we do? Love everyone also and pray, because the Lord can remedy it when he wants to: it can be done in a matter of hours. In the meantime, it will be because that brutal, diabolical tribulation is appropriate for us Christians. The devil does not take holidays, and this can be seen right now, for he is acting inside the Church.

7 The Way, 443

The Holy Father Paul VI had also warned us publicly of this snare of the devil, who had managed to infiltrate into the Church, trying to make the oldest heresies appear as a defence of the faith, disguising them as something new. Our Founder suffered a lot for the situation of the Mystical Body of Christ. Someone said he was grateful for the doctrinal orientation he received in the Centres to help him do a deeper study. This is part of the work that you will have heard me speak about, and that we try to do with so much love, was the Father’s answer. When the devil makes them (…) tear away the pillars of God’s Church and cast them violently to the floor, we try to go with great love and with much devotion kneel down, kiss that stone, and try to put it back in its place. And for that, we try to place it in the heads and the hearts of the sons of God. With that material, you can do a lot of good in the whole world. It is the same with those internal publications, where we say nothing about setbacks, which are things of little importance, because – otherwise – if one of them were twisted, it could cause gallons of tears, as I have sometimes said. This would be a silly thing to do. We know that to serve God Our Lord, we will encounter ordinary, normal setbacks and difficulties. In those magazines wonderful things are told, and they are said on purpose, so that you read them and make them part of your life and that of others. They are elements of the Church of God that we collect.

No Mass is the same as that of the following day We had already talked about sincerity earlier, but our Father returned to it: he wanted to underline the importance of this way of assuring the unity of the Work in the storm that was affecting the waters of the Church so much. These were times for counteracting disloyalty with a greater fidelity to Jesus Christ: moments for atoning and for living the commitments of our vocation with greater care. We had to try harder than ever in the fight for sanctity. While we are on earth, we are capable of committing all the horrors and all the errors that have been committed by the greatest criminal. So don’t be surprised by anything. Struggle, always but calmly. If not, we would not be men; we would be – what do I know? – stone statues or something like that. We are flesh and blood. So understand that God Our Lord wants us to be like this, fighting all our lives. And together with sincerity, helping one another, so that we all improve in every virtue: While you make a fraternal correction, you have to love the defects of your brothers. If not, it’s not good. There will then be peaceful co-existence and in your Centres there will be a lot of peace, precisely because we make war from within. Peace is a consequence of war, and of victory. And defeat in one battle does not mean that the war has been lost, but that the following battles have to be won. They speak about peace constantly, and they make us repeat those words of Scripture: ‘peace, peace’, when there is no peace.8 How is there to be peace, if they don’t want to fight? In this country of ours they talk about rebellions, and in other places they say protestors, and I don’t know what other things. But we are the great rebels; in these moments, when they only speak about dirty things that satisfy passions which they don’t want to be subject to the mind, and don’t want to pray to God, and fail to know him. We don’t want to be like animals, we want to pray to God, and we do all we can to have intimate dealings with Him. We rebel against all that dirt; we are not animals, but children of God. The Counsellor was telling the Father that it was time to go. Our Father again expressed his regret for not having time to speak to each one of his sons on his own. But in the Mass, we can meet every day. The Father has to ask you to pray for him, so that he be good and faithful, as I also pray for you. Be united to my prayer and to the intention of my Mass, which for many years has been the universal Church, the Pope and the Work. But this includes a multitude of lights and variations, dresses and uniforms, of colours and sounds. A Mass is never the same as the one of the following day. In any case, be united to the intention of the Father, and God will bless you, for we shall be consummati in unum.9

8 Cf. Jerem 6:14 9 John 17:22 3 December 1972

“At half past one,” we read in the diary of the Roman College of the Holy Cross for the 3rd of October 1972, “in the Galleria della Campana, the Father gave us his blessing and asked us to accompany him on the journey he about to undertake, by continuing to be very closely united to his intentions. Straight away, he went down the Scala Romana towards the garage, with don Alvaro and don Javier. We don’t know how long the trip will last, but we all have a mind to fulfil his request: to keep him company so that much good comes from the trip.” Twenty-five years later it is a great joy to confirm how worthwhile our intense prayer was in response to our Father’s request. On the eve of his departure we had celebrated forty-four years of the Work, and then our Founder left on his way to Pamplona. During the following eight weeks, we learnt of his movements through the letters we received: “the Father is in Bilbao... he is going to Portugal... he will travel from Seville to Valencia...” And the news encouraged us to pray more and more: conversions, people whistling, people coming closer to God... Even so, we lacked the perspective then that time provides so as to appreciate how far the fruits would reach during those two months of catechesis throughout the Iberian peninsula. Since his return to Rome on 30th November, we were really looking forward to having a get-together. We already knew some particulars of the trip, but we wanted to hear our Father tell them to us; particularly so for those recently arrived in Villa Tevere, who had not yet had an opportunity to see him. The long-awaited get-together took place mid-morning on Sunday 3rd of December. As soon as he came into the soggiorno, our Father congratulated all the people named “Javier”, who were celebrating their saint’s day. While we sat down, many also greeted don Javier, to wish him a happy saint’s day. There was no need to ask questions before he started to speak about his stay in Spain and Portugal. We were happy to learn that, thanks to the media team, we would be able to see the get-togethers he had had with large numbers of people. He then referred to a very special present: Your Portuguese brothers are impressive, they have an immense amount of work, both in intensity and extension: beautiful (...). I have come back full of enthusiasm for Portugal. Do you know they have already sent me the soup tureen? It is a very amusing soup tureen, made of earthenware, with many staples. It had been broken – clearly it had belonged to a large family – but repaired in a way it can be used. Amote, amo-te, amo-te has been written on it many times. We heard him on many other occasions refer to that present given to him by his sons in Enxomil, because, he would say, I also see myself like that: like the clay soup tureen, broken and with staples.10 Perhaps being so keen to hear the news, we were a little distracted, just listening, and our Father encouraged us to take part. Don’t you ask anything? Speak or ask, be impertinent. I have told thousands and thousands of people that: be impertinent. Then he explained in broad outlines how those meetings with so many thousands of men and women had taken place: There were many microphones. They asked what they wanted to ask, and I answered pertinently (...). We have carried out a great catechesis with our Lord’s grace. Many confessions have taken place, I have been told; even from people who had not gone to confession for many years, and it was they themselves who mentioned it. Some did so before coming to the get-togethers, others did afterwards. There cannot be a greater joy for a priest than to lead souls to our Lord.

10 From our Father, Crónica, 1973, p. 665

Despite those gatherings with so many people, the atmosphere was as if we were only half a dozen people: a small family. Everything has been very beautifully done. And they took place in the morning and in the afternoon. I have come back a little hoarse. All his gratitude was directed to God, because that huge harvest of visible apostolic fruits had no human explanation. God’s Spirit hovered everywhere, and we have to thank our Lord for that. Your prayer could be felt, the prayer of my daughters and my sons from all over the world. Otherwise it would not have been possible to do such work: no one could have managed to preach for two months, morning and afternoon, despite my seven years; only a young fellow. Father, were there many young people? Sometimes thousands of young fellows or girls, sometimes not so many, because it was better that way. But all very attentive. It is something amazing, in the midst of this general revolution, which seems to me to be calming down already. He then began to give some light brushstrokes about each of the places where he had been, like drawing a first sketch of the painting. Pamplona was the departure point. We went first through Lourdes, as you know. Some of your brothers were waiting for us there. We prayed to our Lady for a while, I drank some of that water, as usual, without asking anything from our Lady, but devoutly. We went to San Sebastian and then to Pamplona. Everything had been made ready there, more or less. They do it wonderfully. Three honoris causa doctorates had to be conferred. Summing up, in very plain language, seven speeches: three from the sponsors, three from the doctors and one from me, at the end. There were too many, and it was going to take very long: now that they are shortening everything in the liturgy, in our dealings with God (...). I told them: this is very long, let us try to shorten it, and they managed to do so. We processed in academic silence, a large crowd, wearing traditional garments, lots of colours, and many members of the Faculty... Excellent! From Pamplona he went to Islabe, the conference centre near Bilbao. Several get-togethers were held there, some with priests. The more numerous ones took place in Gaztelueta, the first school promoted by people of the Work and Co-operators. There he had spoken about how to undertake educational projects: making parents the prime movers, forming people in a climate of freedom, transmitting a truly Christian spirit... He was especially pleased by the professional formation that being given in the evening classes to students who were working during the day. The following stop was Madrid. The large gatherings began there, more than in the North, because in the North there was no suitable place to welcome so many people at the same time. I wanted it always to be in one of our Centres. The largest get-togethers in Madrid took place in an area that used to be in the outskirts before. If you consider that it is now impossible for fifty of those so-called Catholics to come together without arguing, and, there, thousands and thousands of people came... I am not exaggerating in the least, because we don’t exaggerate in Opus Dei either. So, if you consider that all these thousands of people passed through without anything unpleasant taking place, it is quite extraordinary. On the contrary, everything was so cordial, full of family spirit, of fraternity. They had to place loudspeakers pointing towards the street, because there was not enough room for people, despite the venues being very large. But, anyway, I expect they have told you all these things. Having seen so many photographs and films, having read and re-read the published volumes later, we know well what those get-togethers were like. But at that time, our Father had to describe them to us and, even so, we did not quite manage to appreciate it all. I know there were authorities, both civil and otherwise, who sat on the floor. The odd minister, too. We were all equal there. While some workmen were quietly sitting on their seats, they sat on the floor quite happily. Could there be greater fraternity? From Madrid we went to Lisbon, and had to wait there for an hour to take another plane to Oporto. Then we were told that somebody was offering us a private plane. Since it cost us nothing, and the other person could afford it, I thought that it was better to save the time and use the energies we were saving to preach Christ. 118

After his stay at Enxomil, on the way towards Lisbon, our Father paid a visit to our Blessed Lady: From Oporto we went to Fatima. We did not expect what we found there. There were many priests, young men and women: people of all ages. The word had spread that he would visit the Sanctuary and, without anybody being summoned, a small multitude gathered there anxious to accompany him in his prayer. I know that you are curious about a particular point and, before you ask me, I will tell you, Yes, we did meet Sor Lucia. People are not allowed to see her, but don Alvaro and I did. We chatted for an hour and a half, and it was difficult for us to leave, because she would not let us go. He continued to talk about his joy in seeing the intense apostolic work being carried out by his children. We slipped away to Lisbon and had the same surprise everywhere: a great deal of work, very intense work. He took great pleasure in recalling his stay in Portugal, a country for which he had so much affection. He had said in Lisbon: How could I not love this land, which has carried Jesus Christ to the ends of the earth? ¿How could I not love this land, which has had the courage to discover so many countries and take the faith of Christ to them? It is possible to find mines everywhere that contain more dross than in Portugal: here you have pure gold! I congratulate you, because God has blessed you in this way.11 Amusingly, he mentioned the visit he had paid to his “relative”, as he called the holy Queen Elizabeth of Portugal, buried in Coimbra. We have a little blood in common, through a certain doña Sancha, queen of Aragon. We arrived there to find everything had been prepared to allow us go and venerate the remains of that holy Queen. They keep her body in the coffin made for her by Philip IV. Addressing the Portuguese, he concluded: Now, you must not rest on your laurels: you have to continue working. When he got round to telling us about Seville, don Alvaro repeated the question that had been asked by that university student, at the Guadaira Hall of Residence: “Father, apart from study, what other mortification can we offer up?” He evidently did not belong to the Work, our Founder continued, because otherwise he would have known that study is what has to be sanctified, with which one has to sanctify oneself and sanctify others: it is our ordinary professional work, in a word. Therefore, that fellow, for the moment, probably does not belong to the Work; but since he is sincere, I am asking God to give him the grace of a vocation. From Andalucía to Valencia. Our Father was very happy to have returned to that land from which so many faithful have come to the Work, from the very early years. I had never been to La Lloma, he told us. It is very nice, with lots of stairs: rather, not so many stairs, because placed one after another they probably do not amount to a couple of storeys, and here we’re used to climbing eight or nine storeys, perhaps more, from Villa Sacchetti. And between one storey and another, in some of these houses, we have up to seven or nine metres. If I am exaggerating, bear in mind that I’ve been to Andalucia... In any case I won’t exaggerate too much, because I have the counterbalance of my Aragonese and Catalan ascendancy. But there the steps are not all together, rather one climbs a few steps, then another few and, when you least expect it, another seven; then you go somewhere else and you find another five... Just as well that, when climbing a long stretch of stairs, I was lucky enough to find two very attractive grilles and, behind the second one, in a little courtyard, a tile image of St Joseph; and since I have such weakness and feel so much affection for the Holy Patriarch, I’m always looking for ways to engage with him. It seemed to me that I was meeting the man who acted as God’s father on earth, with my Father and Lord, whom I love so much. It made me very happy. He mentioned the fireworks display put on by the people of Valencia, who are so keen on fireworks, and then he returned to the subject of the get-togethers:

11 From our Father, Dos meses de catequesis, I. p. 337

I would speak about whatever people wanted, which they say is quite difficult; but we are used to doing our catechesis, our gatherings, our get-togethers, in this way. Therefore, after almost half a century, it turns out to be quite easy. Our Father always wished to hide and disappear, to pass unnoticed. For that reason an effort was made to avoid the media reporting those large gatherings; and also because they were a family occasion, even though the family was very large. Hardly any news appeared in the press, despite the fact that several communication professionals attended some of the get-togethers. They set up a stage, high and wide, and I walked back and forth during the get-together. I covered many miles, but – it has to be said – always tethered, under control of the custos indignior. May don Javier forgive me, more so since today is his saint’s day; but if the other one is dignior, he has to be the indignior, although we don’t call him that, at least not officially... He kept me tied with a cowbell round my neck – he was referring to the microphone – he would grab the cord and... that was the end of it. So I spoke with relative freedom, because don Javier was holding me by the halter. Amusing? Can’t be helped! But if anyone is not conditioned in this life, let him raise his hand; and if anyone does raise his hand I will tick him off, because it is not true. I will prove to him straight away that he is extremely conditioned, even if he does not belong to Opus Dei. The freedom that can exist on earth, considered in another way – without any kind of conditioning – is an illness called licentiousness; it is anarchy. Catalonia was the last stage of the journey. Having been the most recent, and also, perhaps, because the largest get-togethers took place at Brafa, our Father took longer to relate his time in Barcelona. We stayed in Barcelona all the time required, as we did elsewhere. I welcomed many visitors, many priests too. It was necessary to speak to the priests about the same subjects, because people ask more or less the same things, but catechesis consists in adapting to the needs of the audience: more or less cultured, their personal conditions, and their state of mind. It is not a simple matter: university men and women, tradesmen, married couples, young people of St Raphael, young people who did not belong to the Work, people of all ages. Everywhere I went I paid a visit a convent of enclosed nuns. We received many letters asking us to visit them, because people are good and there are millions of people who love Opus Dei, even though the devil is unhappy with Opus Dei: Deo gratias! (...) We would take some chocolates, on the express condition that they should take them... as a penance. We visited Discalced Carmelites, Augustinians, Cistercians, Poor Clares. In all those places we had a conversation, a little spiritual chat; we made them laugh, and think; we encouraged them, telling them in all truthfulness, that they are the Church’s treasure and that we love them very much. A common subject of his catechesis, as he told us, had been the good manners of piety. Everywhere I played the part of Christ’s juggler. I have shown priests, and sometimes laymen, how to make a genuflection. It is rather daring on my part, being seven years old, doing it first quickly, and then slowly, with love, without leaning on anything (...) In my Catalan land... I have a right to say my Catalan land for many reasons. The last one being that I am an adopted son of Barcelona by unanimous vote of the corporation, years ago. And the first one, because I have quite a few Catalan surnames; the first of my ancestors was dancing around there, from the days of James I, and only established himself in Aragon in the last century. So I am entitled to speak of my Catalan blood. My Catalan blood explains perfectly what happened to us in a convent. It was the only place where the very charming and very good abbess opened the enclosed area for us... We were inside and, to my great surprise, the “boss” told them how many chocolates each one could have. She had counted them – like a good Catalan, she knew how to do the division (...) – and then she told them each one’s share. I had to make an effort not to burst out laughing. She was a very refined person, but she had it in her blood. Even to dance the sardana, we Catalans count! This is an inherited thing. I did not want to ask her for her surname, but one could tell every ounce of her was Catalan. It was very amusing. We laughed heartily at the story while our Father pretended to be surprised: I didn’t know you were going to be so amused! 120

Someone asked if he had been to the Sanctuary of Torreciudad, still under construction. We didn’t know that he had offered the sacrifice of not going there until the works were completed. I didn’t go to see Torreciudad. Torreciudad came to me. Some two hundred persons came from Barbastro to Barcelona (...). Three architects came from Torreciudad, bringing all the drawings imaginable, to show us how the works were getting on. I can certainly say in advance that there are no precedents of this style in the Church: Heliodoro has invented all of it. I praised him for it, as was his due: he has devoted his whole heart to it. Everything will work very well there, because it has all been very carefully studied. What did your compatriots say, Father? They hardly had time to say anything, because they were put in a room where they waited for two and a half hours, as it was impossible to see them any earlier. I could only spend three or four minutes with them. An elderly lady, who occasionally writes to Javi, approached me. You tell it, Javi! Suffice to say that she is the same age as me... She placed herself in the first row. They were wedged tight, like sardines in a tin. Don Javier made us laugh again describing the scene. The lady caught our Father’s attention repeating: “I am Adriana!, I am Adriana!” Our Founder certainly remembered her: We played together many times, when we were small! After all those years, Adriana kept an old “grievance” about his childish mischief: “Yes, you used to pull my hair a lot!” I was a specialist in pulling girls’ hair if they annoyed me, our Father defended himself: mea culpa! Not much of a fault, because one was not very responsible as a child, and they had probably played some dirty trick on me before, or had said something, because women find it easier to say than to do. That unimportant scene of our Father’s childhood was an example of what we have so often heard in his preaching: that the saints have been ordinary people. When they were children they got up to the same mischief as everyone else, and when they grew up they had to fight heroically against the same passions. The catechesis had been an exceptional event, not just for Spain and Portugal. There were also people of the Work who organised trips from other countries to meet our Father. The Americans came twice: very nice, with the same spirit as everyone else, the same family air, the same supernatural outlook. They got a lot of applause, because they said some very nice things (...). They also came from England, France, Germany, Austria and from many other places. Aunt Caroline came, did you know? Let Don Javier tell you. We gave her a present and she was so happy, because she also brought her own little present. Don Javier told us that Aunt Caroline – the elderly lady from London who had come closer to God through her Kelston Club neighbours – had attended two get-togethers and, as we have seen in the film recordings, she claimed her right to climb onto the stage to be closer to our Father, because she was his youngest daughter, for she was only fifteen months old, the time since her conversion. Those thousands of people unanimously said Yes to her, and so did I. She was so happy to go up. The get-together was long and there was time for our Father to touch on other subjects: the need to be humble and sincere, to assimilate well the formation we would receive in the Roman College; the way of dealing with the Most Holy Humanity of Christ... Some of those texts appeared a little later in Crónica. Our Father’s catechesis had not finished and, during our stay in Rome, we would be most direct beneficiaries of his teachings.

São Paulo, 29 May 1974

During his stay in Brazil, as in the other places where he carried out his catechesis throughout the world, our Father devoted many hours to the formation of his daughters and sons, by means of talks and family-type gatherings. A large part of his teachings at the time were published in some volumes of our internal publications, which, under the title Catechesis in America, have done so much good to the faithful of Opus Dei and will continue to do so. Some of these texts, revised by our Father, had been kept with the intention of publishing them later on. Such is the case of those that come to light today in these lines. They bring together some texts of a get-together that our Founder had with his sons on 29th May 1974, at the Centre of the Regional Commission in Brazil. The Father taught us many things and in very different ways. Sometimes he did it by telling us old family stories filled with that homely warmth which forms part, a divine and human part, of the spirit of Opus Dei. On the 29th he spoke to us about the Grandmother, about the affection she had for us; about how afraid he was, as a child, when he took his exams in Lerida; about the Christmas nougat that so delighted Aunt Carmen in her childhood, and about so many other things. How pleasant it is to be in your company! I give thanks to God Our Lord, because I am so at ease, although at the same time there has to be a little bitterness always, something bittersweet; otherwise we would not be human. Here the bittersweet touch is that I should like to speak with each one of you, not just for a while, but for a long time; not for one hour, but for many hours. Recollections from my childhood have come to my memory. I don’t doubt that your mothers would forbid you to go into the kitchen when you were little. I had a special ability to go there, although our mother did not allow us to do so. First of all because all we did was to get in the way and then because we would dip our hand into the chips, and had no appetite later. I bet everyone has pinched chips some time! I also liked to go to the kitchen because Maria was there, an old cook who was an institution in herself: she was part of the family, and she would always tell us the same story, with a fabulous imagination. A story about robbers which made us afraid and which we followed with our eyes wide open. It was always the same. So we would go there and say to her: please tell us the story. We were happy to make do with the same one; we did not want variety. You, my dearest children, are a little like that with the Father. You know very well the things of our spirit, you practise them, you teach them, you get others to practise them, but you like the Father to make a song for you. You know that the most basic aspect of the interior life is to have presence of God: already from the morning we direct our first thought, our first cry, our first clamour; something coming from the heart. Perhaps it is hard at the beginning, but not later on; because with one act, and another and another, a habit, the inclination, is formed. There is a danger that it becomes a purely natural habit, but this does not happen because it is supernatural by its very nature. The first act is to raise the heart to God: My God! May I know how to love you and make you loved, may I have you present in my thoughts, in my desires, in my words, in my deeds. This occurs to all of you, more or less. But one of you said to me: Father, what can I do to have presence of God always? The previous day, the Father had gone for a walk and one of those with him asked him to show him how to keep presence of God. While they were walking, our Founder began to speak about how he would try to have presence of God during the days of the week. I laughed, I laughed a little – trying to make sure the person concerned did not notice – because he is teaching this to the young men who come to the Work, and has explained it many times in what we call St Raphael classes, the Circles. There he has taught them to have presence of God, giving them the alphabet, the spiritual spelling-book. He has advised them, for a start or to begin with, to devote each day to a particular devotion. On Sunday, for instance, it is good to praise the Trinity: glory be to the Father, glory be to the Son, glory be to the Holy Spirit. I usually add: glory be to Holy Mary; and – something child-like, but I don’t mind at all saying it – also to St Joseph. Because, immediately after Mary, Joseph is the most perfect creature. Besides, he loves us so much! He is our Father and Lord, the head of our house: fecit te Deus quasi Patrem Regis, et dominum universae domus eius, we say to him. We are children of that King and we belong to that divine household; therefore we can turn to St Joseph as our Father, as our Lord. None of the prophets or the patriarchs gets annoyed with me if I say that, after Our Lady, comes St Joseph. I am sure you like it and I find it consoling, and it seems reasonable to me. And on Mondays? This is not a command but a piece of advice. Besides, you can add dressing as you please. On Mondays I can pray for the souls in purgatory. We have such dearly beloved souls there... In fact they are in a happy situation, because they are certain of their salvation. They are lacking purification, they are lacking “parsley dressing”, as we would say in our land of Aragon, right, Xavier? Parsley is a little plant which condiments many sauces. When a woman puts on makeup, when she dresses up, when a handsome young man like you turns himself out elegantly, wears a good knot in his tie, well... they say he dresses up with parsley. I was saying, then: Mondays, for example, you can devote to the souls in purgatory. We have so many people there, I’m sure. Besides, we feel friends and brothers of all of them because they have almost reached the laurel crown of Heaven, almost enjoying the Love of God: they lack the last purification. They are good intermediaries to help us conquer in the little things that make up our day’s work. When he reached this point, the Father interrupted the thread of the conversation to speak about the trip he had made the previous day by helicopter to the Sanctuary of our Lady Aparecida, patroness of Brazil, where he prayed with countless daughters and sons of his. It was one of those helicopters with a transparent roof and walls so that one can see the sky, the towns and the fields perfectly well from inside. On the return journey, the pilot flew over São Paulo at low altitude, so the Father could see the city well. Our Founder accepted with pleasure, and used those moments to pray with greater intensity and to chat with the pilot, who became very fond of him. Our Father made a supernatural conclusion from that experience, so that we could apply it to our life: That young man who piloted the helicopter yesterday is very kind. He wanted to take us over the centre of São Paulo, and we could see all those enormous buildings from above; but we could hardly notice the smaller ones: it was wonderful! Well, all are constructed from small things: handfuls of cement, stones from the river, steel... Our life is also made up of little things; but it is something great, because it is divine: it is the life of a son of God, of an apostle of God, Our Lord. But let us return to our week, and how to seek presence of God. Tuesday? I devote it to the Guardian Angels. You may say what you like, because nobody is dictating to you. It is enough for you to repeat the words of the Preces: Sancti Angeli Custodes nostri, defendite nos... Or whatever you wish. Without words! A heart-beat that goes straight... No need to say any more. And Wednesday? It is St Joseph’s day, once again. On Thursday, many acts in honour of the Eucharist: Spiritual communions, acts of atonement... They offend Our Lord now more than ever, in that Sacrament of Love. In the first place some who are his ministers, who unfortunately are quite demented. They are not bad people. I don’t know any bad priests: I know sick priests who do bad things. It hurts me. I wish I could say the opposite, but I would be lying, because they behave badly (...). How much atonement Our Lord needs! also on account of our personal offences. And Friday? On Friday, remember the Cross. The Work is like a great Christian building, which, at the last hour, Our Lord wished to crown with the Cross. Like a great cathedral, such as those built by dint of manual work, because all those builders gave of themselves. They lived modestly in those hamlets in the Middle Ages, yet the cathedral stood out like a great transatlantic liner that still astonishes us in so many places. Generosity! And, at the end, to crown the building, the cross. 123

I had no intention of founding anything. The name Opus Dei is not mine; I tell you this in God’s presence. And the name of the Holy Cross is God’s: I also tell you this in his presence. He wished to crown the Work in this fashion. So, let us love the Cross: I love it! It makes me very happy to carry on me a Lignum Crucis, a small relic of the Holy Cross. The Father opened his cassock a little and showed the Lignum Crucis. I would truly like to worship the Cross. Not only this one: the piece of wood, the log to which Christ Jesus, Our Lord, was stitched with nails, but also the cross of every day, the little setback, the unexpected one, that one which is really and objectively unjust, or so it seems to me, due to my pride. On Fridays, Ave crux spes unica. And we feel a pang of conscience for complaining about some suffering or other. What is our suffering next to our Lord’s? Ave crux! Greet him on the cross, offer him words of love: I won’t flee from you, I’ll embrace you. As soon as we embrace the Cross and love it, there is no longer any difficulty, dishonour, calumny, slander, sickness. Everything becomes agreeable, nothing is burdensome. For the Cross is not yours or mine but Christ’s. As I wrote to you many years ago about my poor but abundant experience as a priest: when it is He, Christ, who bears the Cross, it no longer weighs on us. How good it is on Fridays to remember the Cross of Christ! And Saturday? La Madonna! as the Italians say. Our Mother in Heaven, Our Lady! I was greatly consoled to see the love, the faith, with which all of you prayed up there; at that sanctuary where devotion is truly very spiritual, because one can hardly see the image. Even from close up one could hardly see it. What will you say to the Virgin on Saturday? It will depend on the circumstances of each one, and it will not be the same thing every Saturday. We had all been following our Father’s words with great attention, while prayer came naturally and we tried to engrave in our souls even the inflections of his voice. How many times I have brought them to mind since then, to meditate on them once again and bring new light to them! Because, as our Founder said well in that unforgettable get-together, we like to hear again and again the story that delights us, the one that our most beloved Father knew how to explain to us with words and intonations that were always new. On that occasion, he ended his teaching like this: Father, you will say to me, but should we do exactly like this? No! I have told you what they used to do with me as a child, when I was little, and I had to learn to write. I would start here and end there: nothing came out in a straight line. Then they would place a template underneath, to prevent me from writing crooked. Nowadays they don’t do it like this anymore; it can’t be helped! In my days they would make us do pothooks to bursting point. I used to do ink blots, many blots; I have always been mischievous. Despite being Escrivá! So, I have given you the template, as they gave me when I was little, so that I would learn to write straight. But, after that... My handwriting was not the same as the teacher’s, even when the teacher would lead me by the hand and guided me. It was awkward because you had to place your thumb here, the other one there, and the nib holder in the middle, which held a crown-shaped nib. One had to hold it properly and not dip your fingers in the inkwell. Another problem! In the matter of the inkwell I did not go as far as one of your sisters, who would bet with her fellow pupils to see who would drink the most ink at one go... Then, my handwriting was rather shaky, like that of all children. Perhaps it had some similarity with the teacher’s; but a few years later it was already my handwriting and did not resemble the teacher’s handwriting in any way. My children: when I suggest something to you, telling you the same story, like Maria the cook, you will not go and do literally what I tell you; instead each one will compose his own story, each one will be unafraid to find his own familiar way along God’s paths. Is that clear? Yes, Father, we all answered. What no one should do is to be unconcerned about presence of God. If he has no presence of God, he will not do well: he will not be mortified, he will not be spiritual, he will not be zealous, he will not feel like working. This one looks at me as if to say: I already knew the story... But you like me to retell it; so that you, in your turn, may retell it with your example and with your word, living it better than me, to so many thousands of souls in this blessed land of Brazil or in other lands, because your ambition is for the whole world. 124
